### I / I

===Original===
The moving was over and done. Professor St. Peter was alone in the dismantled house where he had lived ever since his marriage, where he had worked out his career and brought up his two daughters. It was almost as ugly as it is possible for a house to be; square, three stories in height, painted the colour of ashes—the front porch just too narrow for comfort, with a slanting floor and sagging steps. As he walked slowly about the empty, echoing rooms on that bright September morning, the Professor regarded thoughtfully the needless inconveniences he had put up with for so long; the stairs that were too steep, the halls that were too cramped, the awkward oak mantles with thick round posts crowned by bumptious wooden balls, over green-tiled fireplaces.
Certain wobbly stair treads, certain creaky boards in the upstairs hall, had made him wince many times a day for twenty-odd years—and they still creaked and wobbled. He had a deft hand with tools, he could easily have fixed them, but there were always so many things to fix, and there was not time enough to go round. He went into the kitchen, where he had carpentered under a succession of cooks, went up to the bathroom on the second floor, where there was only a painted tin tub; the taps were so old that no plumber could ever screw them tight enough to stop the drip, the window could only be coaxed up and down by wriggling, and the doors of the linen closet didn’t fit. He had sympathized with his daughters’ dissatisfaction, though he could never quite agree with them that the bath should be the most attractive room in the house. He had spent the happiest years of his youth in a house at Versailles where it distinctly was not, and he had known many charming people who had no bath at all. However, as his wife said: “If your country has contributed one thing, at least, to civilization, why not have it?” Many a night, after blowing out his study lamp, he had leaped into that tub, clad in his pyjamas, to give it another coat of some one of the many paints that were advertised to behave like porcelain, and didn’t.

===Chinese===
搬家的事，结束并了结了。圣彼得教授（Professor St. Peter）独自留在这栋搬空了的房子里——自打结婚以来他便住在这里，他的事业是在这里做出来的，他的两个女儿也是在这里养大的。这房子简直丑得不能再丑了；方方正正，三层高，漆成灰烬的颜色——前廊窄得只差那么一点就不舒服，地板倾斜，台阶下陷。九月一个晴朗的早晨，教授在空荡荡、回声阵阵的房间里缓步踱着，若有所思地打量着他这么多年来忍受的那些毫无必要的种种不便：太陡的楼梯，太逼仄的门厅，那些笨重的橡木壁炉架，配着粗圆柱子，柱头是一颗颗趾高气扬的木球，立在贴绿瓷砖的壁炉之上。
有几种摇摇晃晃的楼梯踏板，楼上走廊里有几块吱呀作响的地板，二十多年来一天里不知叫他暗暗皱眉多少回——它们至今还是吱呀作响、摇摇晃晃。他手很巧，会摆弄工具，本可以轻易修好，可要修的东西总是那么多，时间却总也不够分。他走进厨房——他曾在那里，在一个接一个厨娘的锅铲之下做木工活；又上了二楼的浴室，那里只有一只漆过的白铁澡盆；水龙头旧得没有哪个管子工能把它拧紧到不滴水，窗户只能靠扭来扭去才哄得它上下滑动，而放床单布巾的壁橱门则根本合不上。他对女儿们的不满向来是同情的，只是总没法完全同意她们——觉得浴室理应是屋里最招人喜爱的一间。他年轻时最幸福的几年是在凡尔赛（Versailles）的一所房子里度过的，那里的浴室分明就不是如此；他也结识过许多可爱的人，他们压根儿就没有浴室。不过，正如他妻子所说：「既然你的祖国至少对文明贡献过一样东西，何不用着呢？」有许许多多个夜晚，他吹熄书房的灯，穿着睡衣，跳进那只澡盆，给它再刷上一层漆——那些广告里说刷上去会像瓷釉一样的种种漆里的一种——可它偏不像。

===Original===
The Professor in pyjamas was not an unpleasant sight; for looks, the fewer clothes he had on, the better. Anything that clung to his body showed it to be built upon extremely good bones, with the slender hips and springy shoulders of a tireless swimmer. Though he was born on Lake Michigan, of mixed stock (Canadian French on one side, and American farmers on the other), St. Peter was commonly said to look like a Spaniard. That was possibly because he had been in Spain a good deal, and was an authority on certain phases of Spanish history. He had a long brown face, with an oval chin over which he wore a close-trimmed Van Dyke, like a tuft of shiny black fur. With this silky, very black hair, he had a tawny skin with gold lights in it, a hawk nose, and hawk-like eyes—brown and gold and green. They were set in ample cavities, with plenty of room to move about, under thick, curly, black eyebrows that turned up sharply at the outer ends, like military moustaches. His wicked-looking eyebrows made his students call him Mephistopheles—and there was no evading the searching eyes underneath them; eyes that in a flash could pick out a friend or an unusual stranger from a throng. They had lost none of their fire, though just now the man behind them was feeling a diminution of ardour.
His daughter Kathleen, who had done several successful studies of him in watercolour, had once said:—“The thing that really makes Papa handsome is the modelling of his head between the top of his ear and his crown; it is quite the best thing about him.” That part of his head was high, polished, hard as bronze, and the close-growing black hair threw off a streak of light along the rounded ridge where the skull was fullest. The mould of his head on the side was so individual and definite, so far from casual, that it was more like a statue’s head than a man’s.

===Chinese===
教授穿着睡衣倒不难看；论仪表，他身上的衣服越少，倒越发好看。凡是贴身的衣物，都显出他的身架是搭在极好的骨骼之上的——腰胯瘦削，双肩富有弹性，像一个不知疲倦的游泳者。圣彼得虽生于密歇根湖（Lake Michigan）之畔，血统驳杂（一边是加拿大法裔，一边是美国农户），可人们一般都说他生得像个西班牙人。这也许是因为他常年在西班牙，是西班牙史某些方面公认的权威。他有一张瘦长的棕色面孔，下巴椭圆，下巴上蓄着修剪得很贴的范戴克式短须，像一小簇亮亮的黑毛。配着这一头丝绸般极黑的头发，他有一副带金色光泽的褐色皮肤，一只鹰钩鼻，和一双鹰一样的眼睛——棕色、金色、绿色兼而有之。这双眼睛深深嵌在宽大的眼窝里，有充足的转动余地，上面覆着浓密、卷曲的黑眉毛——眉梢在两端猛地向上挑起，像两撇军人的八字胡。他这对凶相的眉毛使学生们管他叫梅菲斯托费勒斯（Mephistopheles）——而眉下那双审视的眼睛，是谁也躲不过的；这双眼睛能在人群里一眼挑出一个朋友、或一个不寻常的陌生人。它们一点也没失了锋芒——虽说此刻，眼睛背后的那个人正感到心头那股热劲在消退。
他的女儿凯瑟琳（Kathleen）曾用水彩为他画过几幅相当成功的写生，有一次说道：「真正让爸爸好看的，是他耳朵上方到头顶之间那段头颅的造型；那可算是他全身上下最好的地方了。」他那部分头颅高高的，光洁，硬得像青铜，贴着头皮生长的黑发在那颅骨最饱满的圆脊上投出一道亮光。他侧面头颅的轮廓如此独特而分明，如此毫不苟且，倒更像一尊雕像的头，而不像一个活人的。

===Original===
From one of the dismantled windows the Professor happened to look out into his back garden, and at that cheerful sight he went quickly downstairs and escaped from the dusty air and brutal light of the empty rooms.
His walled-in garden had been the comfort of his life—and it was the one thing his neighbours held against him. He started to make it soon after the birth of his first daughter, when his wife began to be unreasonable about his spending so much time at the lake and on the tennis court. In this undertaking he got help and encouragement from his landlord, a retired German farmer, good-natured and lenient about everything but spending money. If the Professor happened to have a new baby at home, or a faculty dinner, or an illness in the family, or any unusual expense, Appelhoff cheerfully waited for the rent; but pay for repairs he would not. When it was a question of the garden, however, the old man sometimes stretched a point. He helped his tenant with seeds and slips and sound advice, and with his twisted old back. He even spent a little money to bear half the expense of the stucco wall.
The Professor had succeeded in making a French garden in Hamilton. There was not a blade of grass; it was a tidy half-acre of glistening gravel and glistening shrubs and bright flowers. There were trees, of course; a spreading horse-chestnut, a row of slender Lombardy poplars at the back, along the white wall, and in the middle two symmetrical, round-topped linden-trees. Masses of greenbrier grew in the corners, the prickly stems interwoven and clipped until they were like great bushes. There was a bed for salad herbs. Salmon-pink geraniums dripped over the wall. The French marigolds and dahlias were just now at their best—such dahlias as no one else in Hamilton could grow. St. Peter had tended this bit of ground for over twenty years, and had got the upper hand of it. In the spring, when homesickness for other lands and the fret of things unaccomplished awoke, he worked off his discontent here. In the long hot summers, when he could not go abroad, he stayed at home with his garden, sending his wife and daughters to Colorado to escape the humid prairie heat, so nourishing to wheat and corn, so exhausting to human beings. In those months when he was a bachelor again, he brought down his books and papers and worked in a deck chair under the linden-trees; breakfasted and lunched and had his tea in the garden. And it was there he and Tom Outland used to sit and talk half through the warm, soft nights.

===Chinese===
从一扇已卸去窗帘的窗户里，教授偶然朝他的后花园望了一眼，见了这叫人愉悦的景象，他便快步下楼，逃离了那些空房间里满是尘灰的空气和刺目的光线。
他这围起来的花园，是他一生的慰藉——而这也正是邻居们唯一要非议他的地方。他开始经营它，是在大女儿出生后不久，那时妻子开始为他总把那么多时间花在湖边和网球场上而闹脾气。这件事他得到了房东的帮助和鼓励——房东是个退休的德国农夫，脾气和善，什么事都好说话，唯独在花钱上不肯让步。教授若恰好家里添了新生婴儿，或有系里的宴会，或家中有人生病，或有任何不寻常的支出，阿佩尔霍夫（Appelhoff）都乐呵呵地等着收房租；可要他出钱修缮，那是万万不能。然而一涉及花园，这老人有时倒肯通融。他帮他的房客出种子、插条和中肯的建议，还搭上他那弯曲佝偻的老背。他甚至花了一点钱，分担了那道灰泥墙的一半费用。
教授竟真的在汉密尔顿（Hamilton）造出了一座法式花园。里面没有一根杂草；那是齐齐整整的半英亩地，闪着光的碎石、闪着光的灌木和明艳的花。当然也有树：一棵舒展的马栗树，后部沿白墙一排细瘦的钻天杨，当中是两棵对称的、圆顶的椴树。四个角落里丛生着绿菝葜，多刺的茎枝交织修剪，长成一大丛一大丛的模样。还有一小畦专种调味的香草。鲑鱼粉红的天竺葵垂挂在墙头。此刻法国万寿菊和大丽花开得正盛——那种大丽花，汉密尔顿没有第二个人能种得出来。圣彼得经营这一小片地已经二十多年，早已驾驭了它。春天，对异乡的思念和未竟之事的烦躁一起醒来时，他便在这里消解他的不快。漫长的酷暑里，当他无法远行，他便留在家里守着他的花园，把妻子女儿送到科罗拉多（Colorado）去躲避草原上潮湿的暑热——那暑热滋养小麦和玉米，却耗损人的元气。在那些他又做回单身汉的月份里，他把书和稿子搬下楼，坐在椴树下的帆布躺椅里工作；早、午两餐和下午茶都在花园里吃。也就是在那里，他和汤姆·奥特兰（Tom Outland）过去常坐到温暖的、温柔的夜深，谈上半宿。

===Original===
On this September morning, however, St. Peter knew that he could not evade the unpleasant effects of change by tarrying among his autumn flowers. He must plunge in like a man, and get used to the feeling that under his workroom there was a dead, empty house. He broke off a geranium blossom, and with it still in his hand went resolutely up two flights of stairs to the third floor where, under the slope of the mansard roof, there was one room still furnished—that is, if it had ever been furnished.
The low ceiling sloped down on three sides, the slant being interrupted on the east by a single square window, swinging outward on hinges and held ajar by a hook in the sill. This was the sole opening for light and air. Walls and ceiling alike were covered with a yellow paper which had once been very ugly, but had faded into inoffensive neutrality. The matting on the floor was worn and scratchy. Against the wall stood an old walnut table, with one leaf up, holding piles of orderly papers. Before it was a cane-backed office chair that turned on a screw. This dark den had for many years been the Professor’s study.
Downstairs, off the back parlour, he had a show study, with roomy shelves where his library was housed, and a proper desk at which he wrote letters. But it was a sham. This was the place where he worked. And not he alone. For three weeks in the fall, and again three in the spring, he shared his cuddy with Augusta, the sewing-woman, niece of his old landlord, a reliable, methodical spinster, a German Catholic and very devout.

===Chinese===
然而这个九月的早晨，圣彼得心里清楚：他没法靠逗留在秋花之间来逃避变迁带来的不快。他得像个男子汉一头扎进去，去习惯一种感觉——在他的工作间底下，是一栋死寂的、空荡荡的房子。他折下一朵天竺葵，把它攥在手里，毅然上了两段楼梯，来到三楼。在复折式屋顶倾斜的下面，有一间屋子还摆着家具——也就是说，如果它算曾摆过家具的话。
低矮的天花板从三面倾斜下来，只在东面被一扇方窗打断——窗子靠铰链向外开启，被窗台上的一个钩子支成半开。这是采光和通风的唯一开口。墙壁和天花板都糊着一种黄色的壁纸，曾经十分难看，如今已褪成无害的中性色。地板上的草席磨得发毛。靠墙放着一张老胡桃木桌，翻起一块活动桌板，上面叠着一摞摞井井有条的稿纸。桌前是一把藤背的、靠螺旋杆旋转的办公椅。这间幽暗的小屋，多年来便是教授的书房。
楼下后客厅的旁边，他有一间做样子的书房，配着宽敞的书架存放他的藏书，还有一张像样的写字台用来写信。可那只是个摆设。这儿才是他干活的地方。而且并非他一个人用。每年秋天有三周、春天又有三周，他把这间斗室与奥古斯塔（Augusta）合用——她是那个缝纫女工，他老房东的侄女，一个可靠、有条不紊的老姑娘，德国天主教徒，极其虔诚。

===Original===
Since Augusta finished her day’s work at five o’clock, and the Professor, on weekdays, worked here only at night, they did not elbow each other too much. Besides, neither was devoid of consideration. Every evening, before she left, Augusta swept up the scraps from the floor, rolled her patterns, closed the sewing-machine, and picked ravellings off the box-couch, so that there would be no threads to stick to the Professor’s old smoking-jacket if he should happen to lie down for a moment in working-hours.
St. Peter, in his turn, when he put out his lamp after midnight, was careful to brush away ashes and tobacco crumbs—smoking was very distasteful to Augusta—and to open the hinged window back as far as it would go, on the second hook, so that the night wind might carry away the smell of his pipe as much as possible. The unfinished dresses which she left hanging on the forms, however, were often so saturated with smoke that he knew she found it a trial to work on them next morning.
These “forms” were the subject of much banter between them. The one which Augusta called “the bust” stood in the darkest corner of the room, upon a high wooden chest in which blankets and winter wraps were yearly stored. It was a headless, armless female torso, covered with strong black cotton, and so richly developed in the part for which it was named that the Professor once explained to Augusta how, in calling it so, she followed a natural law of language, termed, for convenience, metonymy. Augusta enjoyed the Professor when he was risqué, since she was sure of his ultimate delicacy. Though this figure looked so ample and billowy (as if you might lay your head upon its deep-breathing softness and rest safe forever), if you touched it you suffered a severe shock, no matter how many times you had touched it before. It presented the most unsympathetic surface imaginable. Its hardness was not that of wood, which responds to concussion with living vibration and is stimulating to the hand, nor that of felt, which drinks something from the fingers. It was a dead, opaque, lumpy solidity, like chunks of putty, or tightly packed sawdust—very disappointing to the tactile sense, yet somehow always fooling you again. For no matter how often you had bumped up against that torso, you could never believe that contact with it would be as bad as it was.

===Chinese===
奥古斯塔每天五点收工，教授平日只在这里工作到深夜，两人并不怎么彼此碍手碍脚。再说，谁也不是不顾及别人的人。每天傍晚，她离开之前，奥古斯塔都会扫起地上的碎布头，卷好她的纸样，盖上缝纫机，再从那张箱式长榻上摘去散落的线头——免得教授在工作时间里偶尔躺上一会儿时，有细线粘上他那件旧晨衣。
轮到圣彼得呢，过了午夜吹熄灯后，他总是小心地把烟灰和烟丝碎屑拂净——抽烟是奥古斯塔很不喜欢的事——并把那扇铰链窗推到第二个钩子能容许的最大开度，好让夜风尽量带走他烟斗的气味。然而她挂在那些模型上的、未做完的衣裳，往往浸透了烟味，他知道她第二天早上再接着做时是要皱眉头的。
这两个「模型」是两人之间常常拿来打趣的话题。奥古斯塔称作「胸像」的那一个，立在屋里最暗的角落，搁在一只高高的木箱上，那箱子里年年存放毯子和冬日的厚外套。那是一个无头无臂的女性半身像，外覆结实的黑棉布，而它得名所据的那一处又发育得格外丰盈——教授有一次向奥古斯塔解释，她这么称呼它，其实遵循了一条语言的自然规律，为方便起见，叫做换喻。奥古斯塔喜欢教授偶尔说点轻佻话，因为她确信他骨子里终究是文雅的。这个身形看上去如此丰腴、如此起伏有致（仿佛你尽可以把头枕在那深呼吸般的柔软之上，永远安息无忧），可你一摸上去，便要受狠狠一惊——无论你摸过多少回。它呈现出所能想象的最无情的表面。它的硬，不是木头的硬（木头以鲜活的颤动回应敲击，触之手上有生气），也不是毡子的硬（毡子会从指上吸去一点什么）。那是一种死寂、浑浊、坑洼的坚硬，像一团团油灰，或紧紧塞实的锯屑——触感极其令人失望，却不知怎的，总能再把人骗一回。因为无论你多少回撞上那个半身躯干，你总不肯相信碰上去竟会那样地糟。

===Original===
The second form was more self-revelatory; a full-length female figure in a smart wire skirt with a trim metal waist line. It had no legs, as one could see all too well, no viscera behind its glistening ribs, and its bosom resembled a strong wire birdcage. But St. Peter contended that it had a nervous system. When Augusta left it clad for the night in a new party dress for Rosamond or Kathleen, it often took on a sprightly, tricky air, as if it were going out for the evening to make a great show of being harum-scarum, giddy, *folle*. It seemed just on the point of tripping downstairs, or on tiptoe, waiting for the waltz to begin. At times the wire lady was most convincing in her pose as a woman of light behaviour, but she never fooled St. Peter. He had his blind spots, but he had never been taken in by one of her kind!
Augusta had somehow got it into her head that these forms were unsuitable companions for one engaged in scholarly pursuits, and she periodically apologized for their presence when she came to install herself and fulfil her “time” at the house.
“Not at all, Augusta,” the Professor had often said. “If they were good enough for Monsieur Bergeret, they are certainly good enough for me.”

===Chinese===
第二个模型则更显得坦露自身；一个全身的女像，穿着一条时髦的钢丝裙，腰身是利落的金属线。它没有腿——这一点谁也看得太清楚——在它亮闪闪的肋骨后面也没有脏腑，它的胸脯像一只结实的铁丝鸟笼。可圣彼得一口咬定它是有神经系统的。每当奥古斯塔夜里给它换上一件为罗莎蒙德（Rosamond）或凯瑟琳赴宴用的新礼服，它便常常摆出一副活泼伶俐的神气，仿佛它也要出门去过夜，要大大地显出一副放浪、轻佻、疯张的样子。它似乎正要踏步下楼，又像踮着脚尖，等着华尔兹起奏。有时这位铁丝淑女把她那举止轻浮的女子姿态演得惟妙惟肖，可她从来没有骗过圣彼得。他是有他的盲点，但她这路人，他可从来不曾被迷惑过！
奥古斯塔不知怎的，竟认定这些模型对一个做学问的人来说是不大相宜的伴侣，于是每逢她来此驻下、到这宅子里来「值班」时，总要定期为它们的存在表示歉意。
「一点也不，奥古斯塔，」教授常常说，「贝热雷先生（Monsieur Bergeret）用得着它们，那我用着自然也够好了。」

===Original===
This morning, as St. Peter was sitting in his desk chair, looking musingly at the pile of papers before him, the door opened and there stood Augusta herself. How astonishing that he had not heard her heavy, deliberate tread on the now uncarpeted stair!
“Why, Professor St. Peter! I never thought of finding you here, or I’d have knocked. I guess we will have to do our moving together.”
St. Peter had risen—Augusta loved his manners—but he offered her the sewing-machine chair and resumed his seat.
“Sit down, Augusta, and we’ll talk it over. I’m not moving just yet—don’t want to disturb all my papers. I’m staying on until I finish a piece of writing. I’ve seen your uncle about it. I’ll work here, and board at the new house. But this is confidential. If it were noised about, people might begin to say that Mrs. St. Peter and I had—how do they put it, parted, separated?”
Augusta dropped her eyes in an indulgent smile. “I think people in your station would say separated.”
“Exactly; a good scientific term, too. Well, we haven’t, you know. But I’m going to write on here for a while.”
“Very well, sir. And I won’t always be getting in your way now. In the new house you have a beautiful study downstairs, and I have a light, airy room on the third floor.”
“Where you won’t smell smoke, eh?”
“Oh, Professor, I never really minded!” Augusta spoke with feeling. She rose and took up the black bust in her long arms.

===Chinese===
这天早晨，圣彼得正坐在办公椅上，出神地望着面前那摞稿子，门开了，奥古斯塔本人就站在那儿。真奇怪，他竟没听见她那沉重而从容的脚步踩在如今已揭去地毯的楼梯上！
「哎呀，圣彼得教授！我可没想到会在这儿碰见您，要不然我就敲门了。看来咱俩得一块儿搬家啦。」
圣彼得已经站起身——奥古斯塔一向喜欢他的礼数——但他把缝纫机前那张椅子让给了她，自己重新坐下。
「坐下，奥古斯塔，咱们商量商量。我这阵子还不搬——不愿把我这些稿子都翻乱。我要待到写完一篇东西。这事我跟您舅舅说过了。我就在这儿工作，到新宅那边去搭伙吃饭。不过这事儿要保密。万一传扬出去，人家兴许就要说，圣彼得太太（Mrs. St. Peter）和我——他们怎么说来着——分居啦、决裂啦？」
奥古斯塔垂下眼，纵容地笑了笑。「我想，您这种身份的人，一般会说『分居』。」
「一点不错；还是个挺科学的词儿呢。嗯，我们并没有。不过我打算在这儿再写一阵子。」
「好的，先生。往后我也不至于总碍着您了。新宅楼下您有一间漂亮的书房，我在三楼有一间又亮堂又通风的屋子。」
「那屋子里可闻不见烟味儿了吧，嗯？」
「嗳，教授，我其实压根儿不在意！」奥古斯塔动情地说。她站起来，用她那双长臂抱起了那尊黑色的胸像。

===Original===
The Professor also rose, very quickly. “What are you doing?”
She laughed. “Oh, I’m not going to carry them through the street, Professor! The grocery boy is downstairs with his cart, to wheel them over.”
“Wheel them over?”
“Why, yes, to the new house, Professor. I’ve come a week before my regular time, to make curtains and hem linen for Mrs. St. Peter. I’ll take everything over this morning except the sewing-machine—that’s too heavy for the cart, so the boy will come back for it with the delivery wagon. Would you just open the door for me, please?”
“No, I won’t! Not at all. You don’t need her to make curtains. I can’t have this room changed if I’m going to work here. He can take the sewing-machine—yes. But put her back on the chest where she belongs, please. She does very well there.” St. Peter had got to the door, and stood with his back against it.
Augusta rested her burden on the edge of the chest.
“But next week I’ll be working on Mrs. St. Peter’s clothes, and I’ll need the forms. As the boy’s here, he’ll just wheel them over,” she said soothingly.
“I’m damned if he will! They shan’t be wheeled. They stay right there in their own place. You shan’t take away my ladies. I never heard of such a thing!”
Augusta was vexed with him now, and a little ashamed of him. “But, Professor, I can’t work without my forms. They’ve been in your way all these years, and you’ve always complained of them, so don’t be contrary, sir.”

===Chinese===
教授也立刻站了起来，动作很快。「您干什么？」
她笑了。「嗳，我总不会从街上抱着它们走，教授！杂货铺的小伙子带着车在楼下呢，把它们推过去。」
「推过去？」
「是啊，推到新宅去呀，教授。我比平日早来了一星期，给圣彼得太太做窗帘、给亚麻布品缝边呢。今天上午我什么都搬过去，只除了缝纫机——它太重，小推车装不下，让那小伙子回头用送货马车来拉。劳您驾，给我开一下门，行么？」
「不行，我不开！绝对不行。做窗帘用不着她。我要在这儿工作，这屋子就不能改样。缝纫机他可以拉走——可以。可请把她放回箱子上，放回她该待的地方。她在那儿就挺好。」圣彼得已经走到门口，背抵着门站着。
奥古斯塔把手里的重物搁在箱沿上。
「可下星期我就要做圣彼得太太的衣裳了，到时候要用这些模型。小伙子既然来了，让他顺手推过去就是了，」她劝道。
「他敢！休想把她们推走。她们就待在原地，待在她们自己的地方。不许把我的女士们带走。我从来没听说过这种事！」
奥古斯塔这下生了他的气，又有点替他难为情。「可是教授，没有这些模型我没法干活呀。这些年它们一直碍着您的事，您也一直抱怨它们，您就别犯拗了，先生。」

===Original===
“I never complained, Augusta. Perhaps of certain disappointments they recalled, or of cruel biological necessities they imply—but of them individually, never! Go and buy some new ones for remind me when it’s All Souls’ day, or Ember day, or Maundy Thursday, or anything?”
Augusta said she must be leaving. St. Peter heard her well-known tread as she descended the stairs. How much she reminded him of, to be sure! She had been most at the house in the days when his daughters were little girls and needed so many clean frocks. It was in those very years that he was beginning his great work; when the desire to do it and the difficulties attending such a project strove together in his mind like Macbeth’s two spent swimmers—years when he had the courage to say to himself: “I will do this dazzling, this beautiful, this utterly impossible thing!”
During the fifteen years he had been working on his *Spanish Adventurers in North America*, this room had been his centre of operations. There had been delightful excursions and digressions; the two Sabbatical years when he was in Spain studying records, two summers in the Southwest on the trail of his adventurers, another in Old Mexico, dashes to France to see his foster-brothers. But the notes and the records and the ideas always came back to this room. It was here they were digested and sorted, and woven into their proper place in his history.

===Chinese===
「我可从来没抱怨过，奥古斯塔。我抱怨的，也许是它们唤起的种种失望，也许是它们所暗示的残酷的生物学法则——可对它们本人，从来没有！去给提醒我什么时候是万灵节、四季斋日、濯足节之类的日子呀，行么？」
奥古斯塔说她该走了。圣彼得听见她那熟悉的脚步声下楼去。可不，她叫他想起的事可真多！她在这宅子里来得最勤的那些年，正是他女儿们还是小姑娘、需要那么多件干净连衣裙的时候。也正是在那些年，他开始动手写他的大书；那时，要做这件事的渴望和这件事所伴随的种种难处，在他心里相持不下，像麦克白那两个筋疲力尽的泅水者——那是些他有勇气对自己说「我要做成这件炫目的、美丽的、全然不可能的事！」的年头。
在为他的《北美的西班牙冒险家》（*Spanish Adventurers in North America*）伏案的十五年里，这间屋子便是他运筹的中枢。其间有过令人愉快的远行与岔路：两个休学年，他在西班牙查阅档案；两个夏天，他在西南部追寻他那群冒险家的踪迹；还有一年在旧墨西哥；以及临时赶往法国去看望他的义兄们。然而笔记、材料和想法，最终总要回到这间屋子里来。就是在这儿，它们被消化、被分门别类，被织进他的史书里应在的位置。

===Original===
Fairly considered, the sewing-room was the most inconvenient study a man could possibly have, but it was the one place in the house where he could get isolation, insulation from the engaging drama of domestic life. No one was tramping over him, and only a vague sense, generally pleasant, of what went on below came up the narrow stairway. There were certainly no other advantages. The furnace heat did not reach the third floor. There was no way to warm the sewing-room, except by a rusty, round gas stove with no flue—a stove which consumed gas imperfectly and contaminated the air. To remedy this, the window must be left open—otherwise, with the ceiling so low, the air would speedily become unfit to breathe. If the stove were turned down, and the window left open a little way, a sudden gust of wind would blow the wretched thing out altogether, and a deeply absorbed man might be asphyxiated before he knew it. The Professor had found that the best method, in winter, was to turn the gas on full and keep the window wide on the hook, even if he had to put on a leather jacket over his working-coat. By that arrangement he had somehow managed to get air enough to work by.
He wondered now why he had never looked about for a better stove, a newer model; or why he had not at least painted this one, flaky with rust. But he had been able to get on only by neglecting negative comforts. He was by no means an ascetic. He knew that he was terribly selfish about personal pleasures, fought for them. If a thing gave him delight, he got it, if he sold his shirt for it. By doing without many so-called necessities he had managed to have his luxuries. He might, for instance, have had a convenient electric drop-light attached to the socket above his writing-table. Preferably he wrote by a faithful kerosene lamp which he filled and tended himself. But sometimes he found that the oilcan in the closet was empty; then, to get more, he would have had to go down through the house to the cellar, and on his way he would almost surely become interested in what the children were doing, or in what his wife was doing—or he would notice that the kitchen linoleum was breaking under the sink where the maid kicked it up, and he would stop to tack it down. On that perilous journey down through the human house he might lose his mood, his enthusiasm, even his temper. So when the lamp was empty—and that usually occurred when he was in the middle of a most important passage—he jammed an eyeshade on his forehead and worked by the glare of that tormenting pear-shaped bulb, sticking out of the wall on a short curved neck just about four feet above his table. It was hard on eyes even as good as his. But once at his desk, he didn’t dare quit it. He had found that you can train the mind to be active at a fixed time, just as the stomach is trained to be hungry at certain hours of the day.

===Chinese===
平心而论，这间缝纫室简直是一个人所能有的最不方便的书房，可它是整栋屋子里唯一能让他得到独处、能把自己与家庭生活那出引人入胜的戏剧隔开的地方。再没有人踩到他头上来，而楼下所发生的事，沿那道窄楼梯传上来的，只是一种模糊的、通常令人愉快的感觉。除此之外当然再没有别的好处。暖气炉的热气上不到三楼。缝纫室里没法取暖，除了一只生锈的、圆筒形的、没有烟道的煤气炉——这炉子煤气烧得不透，污染空气。要补救这一点，就得把窗子开着——否则，天花板这么低，空气很快就会变得不能呼吸。如果把炉子关小，窗子又只开一条缝，一阵突如其来的风就会把这可怜东西整个吹灭，一个沉浸在工作中的人，说不定还没觉察就已中毒身亡。教授发现，冬天最好的法子，是把煤气开到最大，把窗子用钩子大开着，哪怕他得在工作服外面再套一件皮夹克。靠这种安排，他总算勉强弄到够他工作的空气。
他现在也纳闷，自己怎么从未去找一只好点的炉子、一个新式的型号；或者至少，怎么没把这只锈得一片片起皮的炉子刷上一层漆。可他一向是靠忽略那些消极的舒适才能过活的。他绝不是个苦行者。他知道自己为了个人的乐趣是极其自私的，会为之争夺。如果一件东西令他快乐，他便要弄到手，哪怕为此把衬衣卖了。靠着省去许多所谓的必需品，他才设法拥有了他的奢侈品。比方说，他本可以在写字台上方那个灯座上接一盏方便的吊式电灯。他却宁可点一盏忠实的煤油灯来写东西，那灯是他自己添油、自己照料的。可有的时候，他发现壁橱里的油壶空了；这时要再去打油，他就得穿过整栋屋子下到地窖去；而半路上他几乎一定会被孩子们正在做的事吸引住，或者被他妻子正在做的事吸引住——要不然他就会留心到，洗碗池底下那块漆布又被女佣踢得翘起来、破了，于是停下来把它钉好。在穿过这栋满是人的屋子下楼这趟险象环生的旅程里，他可能丢掉情绪，丢掉热情，甚至丢掉脾气。所以每逢灯里空了——而那又通常发生在他写到一段最要紧的关头的时候——他便把一顶遮光帽檐往额头上一扣，就着那折磨人的、梨形灯泡的光工作。那灯泡从墙上探出一截短短的弯脖子，刚好在他桌面上方约四英尺的地方。对一双像他这样好的眼睛，这也是够受的。可一旦坐到桌前，他就不敢离开了。他发现，人是可以训练自己的头脑在固定时刻活跃起来的，就像训练胃在一天里某些钟点感到饥饿一样。

===Original===
If someone in the family happened to be sick, he didn’t go to his study at all. Two evenings of the week he spent with his wife and daughters, and one evening he and his wife went out to dinner, or to the theatre or a concert. That left him only four. He had Saturdays and Sundays, of course, and on those two days he worked like a miner under a landslide. Augusta was not allowed to come on Saturday, though she was paid for that day. All the while that he was working so fiercely by night, he was earning his living during the day; carrying full university work and feeding himself out to hundreds of students in lectures and consultations. But that was another life.
St. Peter had managed for years to live two lives, both of them very intense. He would willingly have cut down on his university work, would willingly have given his students chaff and sawdust—many instructors had nothing else to give them and got on very well—but his misfortune was that he loved youth—he was weak to it, it kindled him. If there was one eager eye, one doubting, critical mind, one lively curiosity in a whole lecture-room full of commonplace boys and girls, he was its servant. That ardour could command him. It hadn’t worn out with years, this responsiveness, any more than the magnetic currents wear out; it had nothing to do with Time.
But he had burned his candle at both ends to some purpose—he had got what he wanted. By many petty economies of purse, he had managed to be extravagant with not a cent in the world but his professor’s salary—he didn’t, of course, touch his wife’s small income from her father. By eliminations and combinations so many and subtle that it now made his head ache to think of them, he had done full justice to his university lectures, and at the same time carried on an engrossing piece of creative work. A man can do anything if he wishes to enough, St. Peter believed. Desire is creation, is the magical element in that process. If there were an instrument by which to measure desire, one could foretell achievement. He had been able to measure it, roughly, just once, in his student Tom Outland—and he had foretold.

===Chinese===
家里若碰巧有人病了，他就干脆不上书房。一星期里有两个晚上他陪妻子女儿，还有一个晚上他和妻子出门赴宴，或是看戏、听音乐会。这么一来，他就只剩下四个晚上了。星期六、星期日当然是他的，这两天里他像一个被雪崩压住的矿工那样拼了命地干。星期六奥古斯塔不许来，虽然那天的工钱照算。他夜里如此凶猛地工作的同时，白天还在谋生：承担大学的全部课业，在课堂和答疑里把自己分发给成百的学生。可那是另一种生活。
多年来，圣彼得设法同时过着两种生活，两种都极为紧张。他本很乐意削减大学的课业，很乐意给学生一些糠麸和锯末——许多教员给学生的不过是这些，也照样过得不错——可他的不幸在于，他热爱青春——他抵抗不了青春，青春能把他点燃。只要整整一教室平庸的少男少女之中，有一双渴望的眼睛、一个怀疑的、爱批判的头脑、一份活泼的好奇心，他便成了它的仆人。那份热忱可以驱遣他。这种敏锐的反应性并没有随岁月磨灭，正如磁力不会磨损；它与时间无关。
可他这支蜡烛两头烧，到底烧出了名堂——他得到了他想要的。靠钱包上许许多多的小节省，他竟设法奢华起来——世上除了一份教授的薪水，他别无分文——当然，他妻子从父亲那里继承的那点微薄收入，他是分文不动的。靠着那许许多多、如今回想起来都叫人头疼的删汰与组合，他既对得起大学的全部讲义，又同时进行着一项引人入胜的创造性工作。一个人只要足够想，就能做成任何事——圣彼得是这么信的。欲望即创造，是那过程中魔幻的元素。倘若有一件仪器能量度欲望，人就能预卜成就。他曾经量度过一回，粗略地，就在他的学生汤姆·奥特兰身上——而且他预言准了。

===Original===
There was one fine thing about this room that had been the scene of so many defeats and triumphs. From the window he could see, far away, just on the horizon, a long, blue, hazy smear—Lake Michigan, the inland sea of his childhood. Whenever he was tired and dull, when the white pages before him remained blank or were full of scratched-out sentences, then he left his desk, took the train to a little station twelve miles away, and spent a day on the lake with his sailboat; jumping out to swim, floating on his back alongside, then climbing into his boat again.
When he remembered his childhood, he remembered blue water. There were certain human figures against it, of course; his practical, strong-willed Methodist mother, his gentle, weaned-away Catholic father, the old Kanuck grandfather, various brothers and sisters. But the great fact in life, the always possible escape from dullness, was the lake. The sun rose out of it, the day began there; it was like an open door that nobody could shut. The land and all its dreariness could never close in on you. You had only to look at the lake, and you knew you would soon be free. It was the first thing one saw in the morning, across the rugged cow pasture studded with shaggy pines, and it ran through the days like the weather, not a thing thought about, but a part of consciousness itself. When the ice chunks came in of a winter morning, crumbly and white, throwing off gold and rose-coloured reflections from a copper-coloured sun behind the grey clouds, he didn’t observe the detail or know what it was that made him happy; but now, forty years later, he could recall all its aspects perfectly. They had made pictures in him when he was unwilling and unconscious, when his eyes were merely open wide.

===Chinese===
这间见证过那么多败绩与凯旋的屋子，有一样好处。从窗口望出去，他看得见，在很远很远的、就在地平线上的地方，有一道长长的、蓝色的、雾蒙蒙的抹痕——密歇根湖，他童年时那片内陆的海。每当他疲倦迟钝，每当他面前那些白纸依旧空白、或满是划掉的句子，他便离开书桌，坐火车到十二英里外的一个小站，在湖上和他的帆船消磨一整天：跳下水游泳，仰面漂浮在船边，然后再爬回船上去。
当他记起童年，他记起的是蓝色的水。当然，以它为背景的，有几个人物的身影：他讲求实际、意志坚强的卫理公会派母亲，他温和的、日渐淡出天主教的父亲，那个老法裔加祖父，还有各式各样的兄弟姐妹。可生活中的大事实，那种随时都可能从乏味中脱身的逃路，便是这湖。太阳从湖里升起，一天就从那里开始；它像一扇谁也关不上的敞开的门。陆地和它所有的沉闷，永远没法把你围拢。你只需看一眼湖，就知道你很快便会自由。那是清晨第一眼望见的东西，隔着那片长满蓬松松的松树、高低不平的放牛牧场；它像天气一样穿过那些日子，不是一件被想起的事，而是意识本身的一部分。冬日早晨，冰块涌来，碎白一片，从灰云后面那轮古铜色的太阳那里反射出金色和玫瑰色的光，他那时并不曾留意这些细节，也不知是什么让他快活；可如今，四十年后，他却能纤毫毕现地回想出它的一切面貌。它们是在他还不情愿、还不自觉、只是把眼睛睁得大大的的时候，便在他心里成了画的。

===Original===
When he was eight years old, his parents sold the lakeside farm and dragged him and his brothers and sisters out to the wheat lands of central Kansas. St. Peter nearly died of it. Never could he forget the few moments on the train when that sudden, innocent blue across the sand dunes was dying forever from his sight. It was like sinking for the third time. No later anguish, and he had had his share, went so deep or seemed so final. Even in his long, happy student years with the Thierault family in France, that stretch of blue water was the one thing he was homesick for. In the summer he used to go with the Thierault boys to Brittany or to the Languedoc coast; but his lake was itself, as the Channel and the Mediterranean were themselves. “No,” he used to tell the boys, who were always asking him about *le Michigan*, “it is altogether different. It is a sea, and yet it is not salt. It is blue, but quite another blue. Yes, there are clouds and mists and seagulls, but—I don’t know, *il est toujours plus naïf*.”
Afterward, when St. Peter was looking for a professorship, because he was very much in love and must marry at once, out of the several positions offered him he took the one at Hamilton, not because it was the best, but because it seemed to him that any place near the lake was a place where one could live. The sight of it from his study window these many years had been of more assistance than all the convenient things he had done without would have been.

===Chinese===
他八岁那年，父母卖掉了湖边的农场，把他和他的兄弟姐妹们拖到了堪萨斯中部的麦地里。圣彼得几乎为此送了命。他永远忘不了火车上的那几个瞬间——沙丘外那一抹突如其来、天真无邪的蓝色，正从他的眼前永远地消逝。那像第三次沉下去一样。此后的任何痛苦——他也受过不少——都不曾沉得那么深，或显得那么不可挽回。即便是在法国与蒂罗（Thierault）一家共度的那些漫长而幸福的学生时代，那一片蓝色的水，也是他唯一想家的东西。夏天他常和蒂罗家的男孩们到布列塔尼、或到朗格多克的海岸去；可他的湖自有它的样子，正如英吉利海峡和地中海各有各的样子。「不，」他常常对那些总爱追问他《密歇根湖》（*le Michigan*）的男孩们说，「它完全不同。它是海，可它不咸。它是蓝的，却又是另一种蓝。是的，那里也有云、有雾、有海鸥，可是——我也说不清，它永远更天真些（*il est toujours plus naïf*）。」
后来，圣彼得因为热恋着、不得不马上成婚，便四处谋求一个教授职位。在提供给他的好几个位置里，他挑了汉密尔顿的那个，不是因为它最好，而是因为在他看来，凡是靠近这湖的地方，就是一个可以过活的地方。这些年来，从书房窗口望见它，比所有他未曾享用的便利之物加起来，都更有助益。

===Original===
Just in that corner, under Augusta’s archaic “forms,” he had always meant to put the filing-cabinets he had never spared the time or money to buy. They would have held all his notes and pamphlets, and the spasmodic rough drafts of passages far ahead. But he had never got them, and now he really didn’t need them; it would be like locking the stable after the horse is stolen. For the horse was gone—that was the thing he was feeling most just now. In spite of all he’d neglected, he had completed his *Spanish Adventurers* in eight volumes—without filing-cabinets or money or a decent study or a decent stove—and without encouragement, Heaven knew! For all the interest the first three volumes awoke in the world, he might as well have dropped them into Lake Michigan. They had been timidly reviewed by other professors of history, in technical and educational journals. Nobody saw that he was trying to do something quite different—they merely thought he was trying to do the usual thing, and had not succeeded very well. They recommended to him the more even and genial style of John Fiske.

===Chinese===
就在那个角落，奥古斯塔那些古旧的「模型」底下，他本一直想放上几只档案柜——可他始终没抽出时间、也没抽出钱去买。那些柜子本可以装下他全部的笔记和小册子，还有那些远远超前、断断续续打下的草稿。可他一直没弄到手，而如今他实在也用不着了；那好比马被偷了再锁马厩。因为马已经没了——这正是此刻他感受最深的事。尽管疏忽了那么多，他还是把他的《北美的西班牙冒险家》写完了，整整八卷——没有档案柜、没有钱、没有一间像样的书房、没有一只像样的炉子——而且，老天知道，还没有半点鼓励！前三卷在世上所引起的那点兴趣，他倒不如把它们丢进密歇根湖里去算了。它们被其他历史学教授们胆怯地在学术和教育刊物上评论了一番。没有人看出来他想做的，是一件截然不同的事——他们只以为他在做那件寻常的事，只是做得不太成功罢了。他们还向他推荐约翰·菲斯克（John Fiske）那种更为平稳、更和煦的文笔。

===Original===
St. Peter hadn’t, he could honestly say, cared a whoop—not in those golden days. When the whole plan of his narrative was coming clearer and clearer all the time, when he could feel his hand growing easier with his material, when all the foolish conventions about that kind of writing were falling away and his relation with his work was becoming every day more simple, natural, and happy—he cared as little as the Spanish Adventurers themselves what Professor So-and-So thought about them. With the fourth volume he began to be aware that a few young men, scattered about the United States and England, were intensely interested in his experiment. With the fifth and sixth, they began to express their interest in lectures and in print. The two last volumes brought him a certain international reputation and what were called rewards—among them, the Oxford prize for history, with its five thousand pounds, which had built him the new house into which he did not want to move.
“Godfrey,” his wife had gravely said one day, when she detected an ironical turn in some remark he made about the new house, “is there something you would rather have done with that money than to have built a house with it?”
“Nothing, my dear, nothing. If with that cheque I could have bought back the fun I had writing my history, you’d never have got your house. But one couldn’t get that for twenty thousand dollars. The great pleasures don’t come so cheap. There is nothing else, thank you.”

===Chinese===
圣彼得可以坦白地说，他当时一点也不在乎——至少在那些黄金岁月里不在乎。当整部叙事的全貌一天比一天清晰地浮现，当他感到自己的手对材料越来越游刃有余，当关于那种写作的种种愚蠢的成规一一剥落，他与他作品的关系一天比一天更简单、更自然、也更快乐——某某教授怎么想，他和那些西班牙冒险家本人一样，毫不放在心上。到第四卷，他开始察觉到，美国和英国各地，零零星星有几位年轻人，对他的尝试怀着浓厚的兴趣。到第五、第六卷，他们开始在讲演和印刷品中表达他们的兴趣了。最后两卷给他带来了一定的国际声誉，以及所谓种种酬报——其中有一项，是牛津的史学奖金，五千英镑，正是这笔钱替他盖了那栋他并不想搬进去的新宅。
「戈弗雷（Godfrey），」有一天，他妻子从他关于新宅的某句话里听出了一丝讥讽的意味，便郑重地说，「那笔钱，你可是宁愿拿去做点别的事，也不愿拿来盖房子么？」
「没有，亲爱的，没有。要是凭那张支票能把写我那部史书时的快活买回来，你就永远住不上你的房子了。可两万美元是买不回那个的。那种大的快乐，没有那么便宜。此外别无所求，谢谢你。」

### II / II

===Original===
That evening St. Peter was in the new house, dressing for dinner. His two daughters and their husbands were dining with them, also an English visitor. Mrs. St. Peter heard the shower going as she passed his door. She entered his room and waited until he came out in his bathrobe, rubbing his wet, ink-black hair with a towel.

"Surely you'll admit that you like having your own bath," she said, looking past him into the glittering white cubicle, flooded with electric light, which he had just quitted.

"Whoever said I didn't? But more than anything else, I like my closets. I like having room for all my clothes, without hanging one coat on top of another, and not having to get down on my marrowbones and fumble in dark corners to find my shoes."

"Of course you do. And it's much more dignified, at your age, to have a room of your own."

"It's convenient, certainly, though I hope I'm not so old as to be personally repulsive?" He glanced into the mirror and straightened his shoulders as if he were trying on a coat.

Mrs. St. Peter laughed—a pleasant, easy laugh with genuine amusement in it. "No, you are very handsome, my dear, especially in your bathrobe. You grow better-looking and more intolerant all the time."

===Chinese===
那天傍晚，圣彼得在新宅里换衣服，准备赴晚宴。他的两个女儿和女婿都要来一同进餐，还有一位英国客人。圣彼得太太经过他房门时，听见淋浴的水声。她走进房间，等他裹着浴袍出来，正用毛巾擦那一头湿漉漉、墨一般黑的头发。

"你总得承认，有一间自己的浴室是件痛快事。"她说道，目光越过他，望进他刚刚离开的那个洁白耀眼、被电灯照得通明的小隔间。

"谁说过我不承认了？可我最喜欢的，其实是那几间衣橱。我喜欢地方宽敞，所有的衣服都摆得开，不必一件压一件地挂着，也用不着跪下膝盖，在黑黢黢的角落里摸来找去找鞋子。"

"你当然喜欢。到了你这年纪，有一间属于自己的屋子，也体面得多。"

"方便是方便，不过我总还不至于老得叫人看了生厌吧？"他朝镜子里瞟了一眼，挺了挺肩膀，仿佛在试一件外套合不合身。

圣彼得太太笑了起来——笑得悦耳、从容，带着真切的趣味。"不，你很英俊，亲爱的，尤其穿着浴袍的时候。你是越长越好看，也越长越挑剔了。"

===Original===
"Intolerant?" He put down his shoe and looked up at her. The thing that stuck in his mind constantly was that she was growing more and more intolerant, about everything except her sons-in-law; that she would probably continue to do so, and that he must school himself to bear it.

"I suppose it's a natural process," she went on, "but you ought to try, try seriously, I mean, to curb it where it affects the happiness of your daughters. You are too severe with Scott and Louie. All young men have foolish vanities—you had plenty."

St. Peter sat with his elbows on his knees, leaning forward and playing absently with the tassels of his bathrobe. "Why, Lillian, I have exercised the virtue of patience with those two young men more than with all the thousands of young ruffians who have gone through my classrooms. My forbearance is overstrained, it's gone flat. That's what's the matter with me."

"Oh, Godfrey, how can you be such a poor judge of your own behaviour? But we won't argue about it now. You'll put on your dinner coat? And do try to be sympathetic and agreeable tonight."

===Chinese===
"挑剔？"他放下鞋子，抬头看她。他心里一直搁着一件事：她正变得越来越挑剔，对什么事都挑剔，唯独对两个女婿例外；这情形怕是要一天天继续下去，而他必须设法锻炼自己去忍受。

"我想这是个自然而然的过程，"她接着说，"可你应当努力——我是说，认真努力——在影响女儿们幸福的地方收敛一些。你对斯科特和路易太严了。年轻小伙子都有些愚蠢的虚荣心——当年你自己也不少。"

圣彼得坐着，两肘撑在膝上，身子前倾，心不在焉地摆弄浴袍上的流苏。"嗳，莉莲，我对这两个年轻人所施的耐心，比对我课堂里走过的成千上万个混小子加起来还多。我的忍让已经用力过度，泄了气啦。我的毛病就在这里。"

"哎呀，戈弗雷，你怎么对自己行为的判断力这么差？不过这会儿咱们不争了。你要换上晚餐外套吧？今晚可得尽量和气些、随和些。"

===Original===
Half an hour later Mr. and Mrs. Scott McGregor and Mr. and Mrs. Louie Marsellus arrived, and soon after them the English scholar, Sir Edgar Spilling, so anxious to do the usual thing in America that he wore a morning street suit. He was a gaunt, rugged, large-boned man of fifty, with long legs and arms, a pear-shaped face, and a drooping, prewar moustache. His specialty was Spanish history, and he had come all the way to Hamilton, from his cousin's place in Saskatchewan, to enquire about some of Doctor St. Peter's "sources."

Introductions over, it was the Professor's son-in-law, Louie Marsellus, who took Sir Edgar in hand. He remembered having met in China a Walter Spilling, who was, it turned out, a brother of Sir Edgar. Marsellus had also a brother there, engaged in the silk trade. They exchanged opinions on conditions in the Orient, while young McGregor put on his horn-rimmed spectacles and roamed restlessly up and down the library. The two daughters sat near their mother, listening to the talk about China.

===Chinese===
半小时后，斯科特·麦格雷戈夫妇和路易·马塞勒斯夫妇到了，紧随其后的是英国学者埃德加·斯皮林爵士（Sir Edgar Spilling）——他太想在美国事事入乡随俗，竟穿了一身上街穿的日间常礼服。他是个五十岁的瘦长男人，骨架粗大结实，手脚都很长，脸呈梨形，唇上蓄着战前那种下垂的胡须。他专攻西班牙史，此番专程从他在萨斯喀彻温一位表亲的住处赶到汉密尔顿，是为请教圣彼得博士一些"史料来源"。

介绍完毕，是教授的女婿路易·马塞勒斯主动张罗起斯皮林爵士。他记得在中国见过一位沃尔特·斯皮林，一问，原来是埃德加爵士的兄弟。马塞勒斯自己也有个兄弟在那边，做丝绸生意。两人交换着对东方局势的看法，年轻的麦格雷戈则戴上角质框眼镜，在书房里来来回回焦躁地踱步。两个女儿坐在母亲身边，听他们谈论中国。

===Original===
Mrs. St. Peter was very fair, pink and gold—a pale gold, now that she was becoming a little grey. The tints of her face and hair and lashes were so soft that one did not realize, on first meeting her, how very definitely and decidedly her features were cut, under the smiling infusion of colour. When she was annoyed or tired, the lines became severe. Rosamond, the elder daughter, resembled her mother in feature, though her face was heavier. Her colouring was altogether different; dusky black hair, deep dark eyes, a soft white skin with rich brunette red in her cheeks and lips. Nearly everyone considered Rosamond brilliantly beautiful. Her father, though he was very proud of her, demurred from the general opinion. He thought her too tall, with a rather awkward carriage. She stooped a trifle, and was wide in the hips and shoulders. She had, he sometimes remarked to her mother, exactly the wide femur and flat shoulder-blade of his old slab-sided Kanuck grandfather. For a tree-hewer they were an asset. But St. Peter was very critical. Most people saw only Rosamond's smooth black head and white throat, and the red of her curved lips that was like the duskiness of dark, heavy-scented roses.

===Chinese===
圣彼得太太肤色极浅，是粉红与金黄的调和——如今她头发渐渐有了几丝灰白，那金色也淡了些。她脸上、头发上、睫毛上的色调那么柔和，初见她时，人几乎察觉不到在那一片含笑的色彩之下，她的轮廓其实刻得何等分明、何等果决。一旦她恼了或倦了，那些线条便显出严峻来。大女儿罗莎蒙德在五官上像母亲，只是脸盘略重些。她的肤色截然不同：一头暗黑头发，一双深邃的黑眼睛，柔软白皙的皮肤，双颊和嘴唇透出深褐色的丰润红意。几乎人人都觉得罗莎蒙德美得夺目。她的父亲虽为她十分骄傲，却不同意众人的看法。他觉得她太高，身姿也不大舒展。她略有些含胸，肩和臀都偏宽。她身上——他有时对她母亲说——长的正是他那瘦长身材的法裔加拿大祖父那样宽阔的股骨和平坦的肩胛骨。对一个砍树人来说，那是优点。可圣彼得是挑剔的。多数人看见的只是罗莎蒙德光洁的黑发与白皙的颈项，以及她弯弯嘴唇上的红色，那红色如同色泽浓重、香气馥郁的深色玫瑰在暮色中的颜色。

===Original===
Kathleen, the younger daughter, looked even younger than she was—had the slender, undeveloped figure then very much in vogue. She was pale, with light hazel eyes, and her hair was hazel-coloured with distinctly green glints in it. To her father there was something very charming in the curious shadows her wide cheekbones cast over her cheeks, and in the spirited tilt of her head. Her figure in profile, he used to tell her, looked just like an interrogation point.

Mrs. St. Peter frankly liked having a son-in-law who could tot up acquaintances with Sir Edgar from the Sudan to Alaska. Scott, she saw, was going to be sulky because Sir Edgar and Marsellus were talking about things beyond his little circle of interests. She made no effort to draw him into the conversation, but let him prowl like a restless leopard among the books. The Professor was amiable, but quiet. When the second maid came to the door and signalled that dinner was ready—dinner was signalled, not announced—Mrs. St. Peter took Sir Edgar and guided him to his seat at her right, while the others found their usual places. After they had finished the soup, she had some difficulty in summoning the little maid to take away the plates, and explained to her guest that the electric bell, under the table, wasn't connected as yet—they had been in the new house less than a week, and the trials of building were not over.

===Chinese===
小女儿凯瑟琳看起来比她的实际年龄还年轻——生着当时十分时髦的那种纤瘦、尚未长成的身段。她肤色苍白，一双浅淡的褐眼睛，头发的颜色也是褐色的，其中分明闪着绿色的光泽。在她父亲眼里，她宽阔的颧骨投在脸颊上那一片奇特的阴影，以及她精神勃勃昂起头的姿态，都有一种十分动人的韵味。她侧面的身形，他常对她说，活像一个问号。

圣彼得太太坦白地喜欢有这么一个女婿——能从苏丹到阿拉斯加地跟埃德加爵士一一数出共同的熟人。她看得出，斯科特要闹脾气了，因为埃德加爵士和马塞勒斯谈的那些事超出了他那小小的兴趣圈子。她并不费神去把他拉进谈话，只由他在那些书之间像一头烦躁的豹子似的来回踱步。教授和蔼可亲，却沉默寡言。当第二个女仆来到门口示意晚餐已备好——晚餐是示意，而非通报——圣彼得太太便领着埃德加爵士，引他坐到她右手的位子上，其余的人各自归了常座。喝完汤之后，她费了点劲才把那小女仆叫来撤盘子，便向客人解释说，桌下那个电铃眼下还没接通——他们搬进新宅还不到一星期，盖房子的种种折腾尚未了结。

===Original===
"Oh? Then if I had happened along a fortnight ago I shouldn't have found you here? But it must be very interesting, building your own house and arranging it as you like," he responded.

Marsellus, silenced during the soup, came in with a warm smile and a slight shrug of the shoulders. "Building is the word with us, Sir Edgar, my—oh, isn't it! My wife and I are in the throes of it. We are building a country house, rather an ambitious affair, out on the wooded shores of Lake Michigan. Perhaps you would like to run out in my car and see it? What are your engagements for tomorrow? I can take you out in half an hour, and we can lunch at the Country Club. We have a magnificent site; primeval forest behind us and the lake in front, with our own beach—my father-in-law, you must know, is a formidable swimmer. We've been singularly fortunate in our architect—a young Norwegian, trained in Paris. He's doing us a Norwegian manor house, very harmonious with its setting, just the right thing for rugged pine woods and high headlands."

===Chinese===
"哦？那么要是我早两个星期凑巧过来，就碰不上你们在这儿了？不过，自己盖房子、照自己的心意布置，一定很有意思吧。"他应道。

喝汤时被噤了声的马塞勒斯，此刻带着热切的微笑和微微一耸肩接口道。"盖房子这话对我们正合适，埃德加爵士，我——可不是嘛！我太太和我正为这事苦不堪言。我们正在盖一幢乡间别墅，相当气派的一个工程，就在密歇根湖林木葱茏的湖滨。您也许愿意坐我的汽车去看看？您明天有什么安排？我半小时就能把您送到那儿，咱们可以在乡村俱乐部吃午饭。我们的地段好极了：背后是原始森林，前面临湖，还有自家的沙滩——您要知道，我岳父可是位了不起的游泳健将。我们在建筑师人选上运气奇好——一个年轻的挪威人，在巴黎受过训练。他为我们设计的是一幢挪威式庄园住宅，与四周的环境十分协调，配那粗犷的松林和高峻的岬角正相宜。"

===Original===
Sir Edgar seemed most willing to make this excursion, and allowed Marsellus to fix an hour, greatly to the surprise of McGregor, whose look at his wife implied that he entertained serious doubts whether this baronet with walrus moustaches amounted to much after all.

The engagement made, Louie turned to Mrs. St. Peter. "And won't you come too, Dearest? You haven't been out since we got our wonderful wrought-iron door fittings from Chicago. We found just the right sort of hinge and latch, Sir Edgar, and had all the others copied from it. None of your Colonial glass knobs for us!"

Mrs. St. Peter sighed. Scott and Kathleen had just glass-knobbed their new bungalow throughout, yet she knew Louie didn't mean to hurt their feelings—it was his heedless enthusiasm that made him often say untactful things.

"We've been extremely fortunate in getting all the little things right," Louie was gladly confiding to Sir Edgar. "There's really not a flaw in the conception. I can say that, because I'm a mere onlooker; the whole thing's been done by the Norwegian and my wife and Mrs. St. Peter. And," he put his hand down affectionately upon Mrs. St. Peter's bare arm, "*and* we've named our place! I've already ordered the house stationery. No, Rosamond, I won't keep our little secret any longer. It will please your father, as well as your mother. We call our place 'Outland,' Sir Edgar."

===Chinese===
埃德加爵士似乎极愿意作这一趟出游，便任由马塞勒斯定下时辰，这倒叫麦格雷戈大感意外——他投向妻子的那一眼分明是说，他当真怀疑这位蓄着海象胡的从男爵究竟有多少分量。

约定之后，路易转向圣彼得太太。"您也一起来吧，亲爱的？自从我们从芝加哥弄到那副精巧的铁艺门配件，您还没去看过呢。我们寻到了恰恰合适的一种铰链和门闩，埃德加爵士，又把其余的统统照它仿制。我们可不要那种殖民地式的玻璃门球！"

圣彼得太太叹了口气。斯科特和凯瑟琳刚把他们那幢新平房的门全装上了玻璃球，可她知道路易并非有意刺伤他们——是他那不顾前后的热心，常叫他说出些不识趣的话来。

"我们在所有这些小事上一向运气极好，"路易正乐呵呵地向埃德加爵士吐露。"整个构想真可以说无懈可击。这话我说得出口，因为我不过是个旁观者；全靠那位挪威人、我太太和圣彼得太太一手操办。还有，"他亲昵地把一只手按在圣彼得太太裸露的手臂上，"我们还给我们的地方起了名字！家里的信纸我都已经订制好了。不，罗莎蒙德，我不愿再保守我们这点小秘密啦。这名字会让您父亲高兴，也会让您母亲高兴。我们把我们的地方叫做'奥特兰'（Outland），埃德加爵士。"

===Original===
He dropped the announcement and drew back. His mother-in-law rose to it—Spilling could scarcely be expected to understand.

"How splendid, Louie! A real inspiration."

"Yes, isn't it? I knew that would go to your hearts." The Professor had expressed his emotion only by lifting his heavy, sharply uptwisted eyebrow. "Let me explain, Sir Edgar," Marsellus went on eagerly. "We have named our place for Tom Outland, a brilliant young American scientist and inventor, who was killed in Flanders, fighting with the Foreign Legion, the second year of the war, when he was barely thirty years of age. Before he dashed off to the front, this youngster had discovered the principle of the Outland vacuum, worked out the construction of the Outland engine that is revolutionizing aviation. He not only invented it, but, curiously enough for such a hotheaded fellow, had taken pains to protect it by patent. He had no time to communicate his discovery or to commercialize it—simply bolted to the front and left the most important discovery of his time to take care of itself."

===Chinese===
他抛出这句话，随即退了一步。他的岳母应声接了上去——斯皮林恐怕是听不懂其中含义的。

"多气派，路易！真是灵机一动的好主意。"

"可不是吗？我就知道这名字能打动你们的心。"教授表达自己情绪的方式，只是把那两道浓重、陡然上拧的眉毛抬了一抬。"让我给您解释一下，埃德加爵士，"马塞勒斯热切地接着说，"我们这个地方是为汤姆·奥特兰而命名的。他是一位才华横溢的美国青年科学家和发明家，战争第二年在弗兰德斯随外籍兵团作战时阵亡，当时年仅三十出头。这小伙子在奔赴前线之前，已经发现了奥特兰真空的原理，设计出了正在使航空业发生革命的奥特兰发动机的构造。他不仅发明了它，而且——对这样一个性子火爆的人来说倒是出奇地难得——还费心用专利把它保护起来。他没来得及公布自己的发现，也没来得及把它投入商业应用——便径直冲上了前线，把他那个时代最重要的一项发明丢在那里，听其自然。"

===Original===
Sir Edgar, fork arrested, looked a trifle dazed. "Am I to understand that you are referring to the inventor of the Outland engine?"

Louie was delighted. "Exactly that! Of course you would know all about it. My wife was young Outland's fiancée—is virtually his widow. Before he went to France he made a will in her favour; he had no living relatives, indeed. Toward the close of the war we began to sense the importance of what Outland had been doing in his laboratory—I am an electrical engineer by profession. We called in the assistance of experts and got the idea over from the laboratory to the trade. The monetary returns have been and are, of course, large."

While Louie paused long enough to have some intercourse with the roast before it was taken away, Sir Edgar remarked that he himself had been in the Air Service during the war, in the construction department, and that it was most extraordinary to come thus by chance upon the genesis of the Outland engine.

===Chinese===
埃德加爵士餐叉停在半空，神情有几分发怔。"我是否可以理解为，您指的就是那位奥特兰发动机的发明者？"

路易喜出望外。"正是！您当然对此了如指掌。我太太当年就是小奥特兰的未婚妻——实际上可说是他的遗孀。他去法国之前立下了一份有利于她的遗嘱；他实在没有一个在世的亲人了。临近战争结束时，我们才开始意识到奥特兰在其实验室里所做的工作的重要性——我本行是电气工程师。我们请了专家协助，把那构想从实验室搬进了实业界。所带来的经济收益，过去和现在当然都颇为可观。"

路易趁空停下，赶在被撤走之前对付了一阵那道烤肉。埃德加爵士则说起自己战时也在航空兵部队服役，在制造部门，能这样偶然地撞见奥特兰发动机的起源，实在非同寻常。

===Original===
"You see," Louie told him, "Outland got nothing out of it but death and glory. Naturally, we feel terribly indebted. We feel it's our first duty in life to use that money as he would have wished—we've endowed scholarships in his own university here, and that sort of thing. But our house we want to have as a sort of memorial to him. We are going to transfer his laboratory there, if the university will permit—all the apparatus he worked with. We have a room for his library and pictures. When his brother scientists come to Hamilton to look him up, to get information about him, as they are doing now already, at Outland they will find his books and instruments, all the sources of his inspiration."

"Even Rosamond," murmured McGregor, his eyes upon his cool green salad. He was struggling with a desire to shout to the Britisher that Marsellus had never so much as seen Tom Outland, while he, McGregor, had been his classmate and friend.

===Chinese===
"您要知道，"路易对他说，"奥特兰从这件事里得到的，除了死亡和荣誉之外别无所有。我们自然觉得亏欠得厉害。我们觉得，自己一生头一桩本分，就是照他生前所希望的那样去使用那笔钱——我们已在他自己的这所大学里设立了奖学金，诸如此类。而我们的房子，我们希望把它当作某种纪念他的所在。如果大学方面允许，我们打算把他的实验室整个搬到那里去——连他工作用过的全部仪器。我们还专门设了一间屋子，安放他的藏书和画像。等他的科学家同仁到汉密尔顿来寻访他、打听他的事迹——他们眼下已经在这样做了——在奥特兰，他们将找到他的书籍和仪器，找到他全部灵感的源头。"

"连罗莎蒙德也是。"麦格雷戈喃喃道，眼睛盯着自己那盘清凉的绿色沙拉。他正强忍着一股冲动，想对那个英国人嚷出真相：马塞勒斯连见都没见过汤姆·奥特兰，而他麦格雷戈，才是奥特兰的同窗与挚友。

===Original===
Sir Edgar was as much interested as he was mystified. He had come here to talk about manuscripts shut up in certain mouldering monasteries in Spain, but he had almost forgotten them in the turn the conversation had taken. He was genuinely interested in aviation and all its problems. He asked few questions, and his comments were almost entirely limited to the single exclamation, "Oh!" But this, from his lips, could mean a great many things; indifference, sharp interrogation, sympathetic interest, the nervousness of a modest man on hearing disclosures of a delicately personal nature. McGregor, before the others had finished dessert, drew a big cigar from his pocket and lit it at one of the table candles, as the horridest thing he could think of to do.

When they left the dining-room, St. Peter, who had scarcely spoken during dinner, took Sir Edgar's arm and said to his wife: "If you will excuse us, my dear, we have some technical matters to discuss." Leading his guest into the library, he shut the door.

===Chinese===
埃德加爵士既觉着迷，又觉困惑。他本是来谈论那些封存在西班牙某些霉朽修道院里的手稿的，可话头一转，他几乎把它们忘了个干净。他对航空及与之相关的一切问题都怀着由衷的兴趣。他很少发问，他的评语也几乎只限于一声惊叹："哦！"可这一声"哦"，从他的唇间说出来，却能意味着许多种意思：淡漠、锐利的诘问、感同身受的关切，或是一个谦逊的人在听到涉及私密之事时的局促。其余人还没用完甜点，麦格雷戈已从口袋里掏出一支大雪茄，就着桌上一支蜡烛点燃了——这是他能想到的最煞风景的一招。

离开餐厅时，整晚晚餐上几乎没怎么开口的圣彼得，挽起埃德加爵士的胳膊，对妻子说："亲爱的，失陪了，我们有些学术上的事要谈。"他把客人领进书房，随手关上了门。

===Original===
Marsellus looked distinctly disappointed. He stood gazing wistfully after them, like a little boy told to go to bed. Louie's eyes were vividly blue, like hot sapphires, but the rest of his face had little colour—he was a rather mackerel-tinted man. Only his eyes, and his quick, impetuous movements, gave out the zest for life with which he was always bubbling. There was nothing Semitic about his countenance except his nose—that took the lead. It was not at all an unpleasing feature, but it grew out of his face with masterful strength, well-rooted, like a vigorous oak-tree growing out of a hillside.

Mrs. St. Peter, always concerned for Louie, asked him to come and look at the new rug in her bedroom. This revived him; he took her arm, and they went upstairs together.

McGregor was left with the two sisters. "Outland, outlandish!" he muttered, while he fumbled about for an ashtray. Rosamond pretended not to hear him, but the dusky red on her cheeks crept a little farther toward her ears.

===Chinese===
马塞勒斯明显地露出失望之色。他站在那里，怅然地望着他们的背影，活像一个被吩咐去睡觉的小男孩。路易的眼睛是鲜亮的蓝色，像灼热的蓝宝石，可他脸上其余部分却没多少血色——他是个略带鲭鱼般青黑色调的男人。唯有那双眼睛，和他那敏捷、急促的举动，泄露着他身上始终汩汩涌动的对生活的热切。他的容貌上没有什么闪族特征，除了那只鼻子——鼻子倒是当仁不让。这倒绝非一个惹人厌的特征，它只是以一种强悍的力量从他的脸上长出来，根基深厚，好似一株茁壮的橡树从山坡上长出。

圣彼得太太一向关心路易，便请他过去看看她卧室里那块新地毯。这一下又叫他来了精神；他挽起她的手臂，两人一同上了楼。

麦格雷戈和两姐妹留了下来。"奥特兰，奥特兰ish（古怪）！"他咕哝着，手忙脚乱地四处找烟灰缸。罗莎蒙德装作没听见，可她双颊上那片暗红却悄悄朝耳根蔓延开去。

===Original===
"Remember, we are leaving early, Scott," said Kathleen. "You have to finish your editorial tonight."

"Surely you don't make him work at night, too?" Rosamond asked. "Doesn't he have to rest his brain sometimes? Humour is always better if it's spontaneous."

"Oh, that's the trouble with me," Scott assured her. "Unless I keep my nose to the grindstone, I'm too damned spontaneous and tell the truth, and the public won't stand for it. It's not an editorial I have to finish, it's the daily prose poem I do for the syndicate, for which I get twenty-five beans. This is the motif:

> 'When your pocket is under-moneyed and your fancy is over-girled, you'll have to admit while you're cursing it, it's a mighty darned good old world.'

Bang, bang!"

He threw his cigar-end savagely into the fireplace. He knew that Rosamond detested his editorials and his jingles. She had fastidious taste in literature, like her mother—though he didn't think she had half the general intelligence of his wife. She also, now that she was Tom Outland's heir, detested to hear sums of money mentioned, especially small sums.

===Chinese===
"别忘了，我们得早走，斯科特，"凯瑟琳说，"你今晚还得赶完那篇社论。"

"你总不至于连晚上也逼着他干活吧？"罗莎蒙德问，"他难道有时不需要让脑子歇一歇？幽默这东西，总是自然流露出来的才好。"

"嗳，这正是我的毛病，"斯科特向她保证，"要不是我死死把鼻子按在磨盘上，我就未免太'自然流露'，要说出大实话来，公众可受不了。我要赶完的倒不是社论，是我替报业 syndicate 写的那篇每日散文诗，能挣二十五块大洋。主旨是这样的：

> '当你口袋里缺了银钱，心里头姑娘又太多，你骂归骂，也只得承认，这到底还是个顶呱呱的好世界。'

砰，砰！"

他把雪茄头狠狠地甩进壁炉。他知道罗莎蒙德厌恶他的社论和他的那些打油诗。她在文学上的趣味极为挑剔，像她母亲一样——不过他觉得，她的总见识还及不上他妻子的一半。况且她如今既是汤姆·奥特兰的继承人，也最讨厌听见人提起钱的数目，尤其是小数目。

===Original===
After the good nights were said, and they were outside the front door, McGregor seized his wife's elbow and rushed her down the walk to the gate where his Ford was parked, breaking out in her ear as they ran: "Now what the hell is a virtual widow? Does he mean a virtuous widow, or the reverseous? Bang, bang!"

===Chinese===
互道了晚安，走到大门外之后，麦格雷戈一把抓住妻子的胳膊，拽着她沿小径一路奔到停着他的福特车的大门边，一边跑一边冲着她的耳朵发作起来："他妈的，'实际上'的寡妇算什么玩意儿？他说的是'有德行'（virtuous）的寡妇呢，还是反着来的？砰，砰！"

### III / III

===Original===
St. Peter awoke the next morning with the wish that he could be transported on his mattress from the new house to the old. But it was Sunday, and on that day his wife always breakfasted with him. There was no way out; they would meet at compt.
When he reached the dining-room Lillian was already at the table, behind the percolator. “Good morning, Godfrey. I hope you had a good night.” Her tone just faintly implied that he hadn’t deserved one.
“Excellent. And you?”
“I had a good conscience.” She smiled ruefully at him. “How can you let yourself be ungracious in your own house?”
“Oh, dear! And I went to sleep happy in the belief that I hadn’t said anything amiss the whole evening.”
“Nor anything aright, that I heard. Your disapproving silence can kill the life of any company.”
“It didn’t seem to, last night. You’re entirely wrong about Marsellus. He doesn’t notice.”
“He’s too polite to *take* notice, but he feels it. He’s very sensitive, under a well-schooled impersonal manner.”
St. Peter laughed. “Nonsense, Lillian! If he were, he couldn’t pick up a dinner party and walk off with it, as he almost always does. I don’t mind when it’s our dinner, but I hate seeing him do it in other people’s houses.”
“Be fair, Godfrey. You know that if you’d once begun to talk about your work in Spain, Louie would have followed it up with enthusiasm. Nobody is prouder of you than he.”
“That’s why I kept quiet. Support can be too able—certainly too fluent.”
“There you are; the dog in the manger! You won’t let him discuss your affairs, and you are annoyed when he talks about his own.”
“I admit I can’t bear it when he talks about Outland as his affair. (I mean Tom, of course, not their confounded place!) This calling it after him passes my comprehension. And Rosamond’s standing for it! It’s brazen impudence.”
Mrs. St. Peter frowned pensively. “I knew you wouldn’t like it, but they were so pleased about it, and their motives are so generous—”
“Hang it, Outland doesn’t need their generosity! They’ve got everything he ought to have had, and the least they can do is to be quiet about it, and not convert his very bones into a personal asset. It all comes down to this, my dear: one likes the florid style, or one doesn’t. You yourself used not to like it. And will you give me some more coffee, please?”
She refilled his cup and handed it across the table. “Nice hands,” he murmured, looking critically at them as he took it, “always such nice hands.”
“Thank you. I dislike floridity when it is beaten up to cover the lack of something, to take the place of something. I never disliked it when it came from exuberance. Then it isn’t floridness, it’s merely strong colour.”
“Very well; some people don’t care for strong colour. It fatigues them.” He folded his napkin. “Now I must be off to my desk.”
“Not quite yet. You never have time to talk to me. Just when did it begin, Godfrey, in the history of manners—that convention that if a man were pleased with his wife or his house or his success, he shouldn’t say so, frankly?” Mrs. St. Peter spoke thoughtfully, as if she had considered this matter before.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，圣彼得醒来，心里巴不得有人连人带床垫把他从新宅搬回旧屋去。可这天是星期日，每逢这一天，他妻子总陪他一起用早餐。躲是躲不过去的；他们总要在餐桌上见面。
他走进餐厅时，莉莲（Lillian）已在桌旁坐着，坐在渗滤式咖啡壶后面。「早安，戈弗雷。昨夜睡得好么？」她的语气里隐隐透着一层意思——他根本不配睡得好。
「好极了。你呢？」
「我良心上过得去。」她对他苦笑了一下。「你怎么会在自己的家里也让自己这么不近人情呢？」
「哎呀，亲爱的！我可是带着一整晚没说过一句失礼话的信念，高高兴兴睡着的。」
「我也没听见你说对一句。你那不以为然的沉默，能把任何聚会上的生气全杀光。」
「昨晚看样子并没有。你完全看错马塞勒斯（Marsellus）了。他并没有察觉。」
「他礼貌太好，不肯流露出来，可他是有所感的。他那训练有素、不偏不倚的风度之下，十分敏感。」
圣彼得笑了。「胡说，莉莲！他要是那样，就不可能像他几乎回回做到的那样，把一桌宴席整个儿拎起来据为己有。宴席是我们的，我倒不介意；可我最看不惯他在别人家里也来这一套。」
「公道点，戈弗雷。你知道，只要你一开口谈你在西班牙的工作，路易（Louie）就会热切地接下去。没有人比他更以你为荣了。」
「所以我才不开口。支持未免太能干了——未免太滔滔不绝了。」
「这不就是了；占着茅坑不拉屎！你不让他谈你的事，等他谈起他自己的，你又恼。」
「我承认，他拿奥特兰（Outland）当作他自己的事来说长道短，我就受不了。（我指的是汤姆，当然不是他们那该死的宅子！）把它命名为那个名字，我实在想不通。还有罗莎蒙德竟然由着他们这么干！简直是无耻之尤。」
圣彼得太太若有所思地皱起眉头。「我就知道你不会喜欢，可他们那么高兴，他们的用意又那么慷慨——」
「得了吧，奥特兰用不着他们的慷慨！他们已经把他本该有的一切都拿去了，他们至少该做的，就是对此闭口不谈，别把他连骨头都化为他们个人的一份资产。归根结底，亲爱的：一个人要么喜欢华丽做作的派头，要么不喜欢。你自己从前也不喜欢的。再给我一点咖啡，好么？」
她替他续上咖啡，隔着桌子递过来。「一双手真好看，」他接过杯子，挑剔地端详着它们，低声说，「总是这么好看的一双手。」
「谢谢。我厌恶那种华而不实，是当它被打起来掩饰某种欠缺、顶替某种东西的时候。我从没厌恶过它出于生命力充沛的时候。那不叫华而不实，那不过是浓烈的色彩。」
「很好；有些人就是不喜欢浓烈的色彩。那会叫他们疲倦。」他叠好餐巾。「现在我得上楼去伏案了。」
「先别急。你从来就没工夫和我说说话。戈弗雷，到底是什么时候、在礼节史上是怎么开始的——那条规矩：一个男人若是对自己的妻子、自己的房子、自己的成就满意，就不该坦白说出来？」圣彼得太太若有所思地说着，仿佛这件事她早就琢磨过。

===Original===
“Oh, it goes back a long way. I rather think it began in the Age of Chivalry—King Arthur’s knights. Whoever it was lived in that time, some feeling grew up that a man should do fine deeds and not speak of them, and that he shouldn’t speak the name of his lady, but sing of her as a Phyllis or a Nicolette. It’s a nice idea, reserve about one’s deepest feelings: keeps them fresh.”
“The Oriental peoples didn’t have an Age of Chivalry. They didn’t need one,” Lillian observed. “And this reserve—it becomes in itself ostentatious, a vainglorious vanity.”
“Oh, my dear, all is vanity! I don’t dispute that. Now I must really go, and I wish I could play the game as well as you do. I have no enthusiasm for being a father-in-law. It’s you who keep the ball rolling. I fully appreciate that.”
“Perhaps,” mused his wife, as he rose, “it’s because you didn’t get the son-in-law you wanted. And yet he was highly coloured, too.”
The Professor made no reply to this. Lillian had been fiercely jealous of Tom Outland. As he left the house, he was reflecting that people who are intensely in love when they marry, and who go on being in love, always meet with something which suddenly or gradually makes a difference. Sometimes it is the children, or the grubbiness of being poor, sometimes a second infatuation. In their own case it had been, curiously enough, his pupil, Tom Outland.

===Chinese===
「哦，这要往回追溯很久远。我倒是觉得，它始于骑士时代——亚瑟王（King Arthur）的骑士们。无论那时生活着的是谁，都生出了一种感觉：一个男人该做漂亮的事，而不要去说它；他不该说出他心上人的名字，而要把她当作一个菲利斯（Phyllis）、一个妮可莱特（Nicolette）来歌唱。这是很好的想法，对自己最深的情感保持缄默：能使它们保持新鲜。」
「东方民族并没有过一个骑士时代。他们也用不着，」莉莲说。「而且这种缄默——它本身就变成了卖弄，一种虚浮的虚荣。」
「哦，亲爱的，凡事皆是虚空！这个我不否认。如今我真该走了，我倒巴不得自己能把这场戏演得像你一样好。我对当一个岳父没什么热情。让这场球一直滚着的，是你。这一点我心领。」
「也许，」他站起身时，妻子沉吟道，「是因为你没娶到你想娶的那个女婿吧。可他也是浓墨重彩的一个人哪。」
教授对此没作回答。莉莲曾经对汤姆·奥特兰嫉妒得厉害。他离开宅子时，一路想着：那些婚前爱得炽烈、婚后仍一直相爱着的人，总会遇上某件事，或骤然或渐次地改变一切。有时是孩子们，有时是贫穷的窘迫，有时是第二次痴情。而就他们俩而言，说来古怪——竟是他的学生，汤姆·奥特兰。

===Original===
St. Peter had met his wife in Paris, when he was but twenty-four, and studying for his doctorate. She too was studying there. French people thought her an English girl because of her gold hair and fair complexion. With her really radiant charm, she had a very interesting mind—but it was quite wrong to call it mind, the connotation was false. What she had was a richly endowed nature that responded strongly to life and art, and very vehement likes and dislikes which were often quite out of all proportion to the trivial object or person that aroused them. Before his marriage, and for years afterward, Lillian’s prejudices, her divinations about people and art (always instinctive and unexplained, but nearly always right), were the most interesting things in St. Peter’s life. When he accepted almost the first position offered him, in order to marry at once, and came to take the chair of European history at Hamilton, he was thrown upon his wife for mental companionship. Most of his colleagues were much older than he, but they were not his equals either in scholarship or in experience of the world. The only other man in the faculty who was carrying on important research work was Doctor Crane, the professor of physics. St. Peter saw a good deal of him, though outside his specialty he was uninteresting—a narrow-minded man, and painfully unattractive. Years ago Crane had begun to suffer from a malady which in time proved incurable, and which now sent him up for an operation periodically. St. Peter had had no friend in Hamilton of whom Lillian could possibly be jealous until Tom Outland came along, so well fitted by nature and early environment to help him with his work on the Spanish Adventurers.

===Chinese===
圣彼得是在巴黎遇见他妻子的，那时他才二十四岁，正在攻读博士学位。她也在那里求学。法国人因为她那一头金发和白皙的肤色，都当她是个英国姑娘。她真正光彩照人的风度之外，还有一副很有意思的头脑——可称之为「头脑」是大错特错的，这个词的含义并不对。她所有的，是一种禀赋丰厚的天性，对生活和艺术反应强烈；还有十分激烈的好恶，这种好恶常常与激起它们的那个无关紧要的事物或人物全不相称。婚前以及婚后的许多年里，莉莲的种种偏见、她对人和艺术的种种直觉（总是出于本能而无法解释，却又几乎总是对的），是圣彼得一生中最有意思的东西。为了立刻成婚，他几乎是接受了提供给他的第一个职位，来到汉密尔顿（Hamilton）担任欧洲史讲席教授，从此，他精神上的伴侣只能仰赖他的妻子。他的大多数同事都比他年长许多，可无论在学问还是见识上，都不是他的对手。系里唯一另一位在从事重要研究工作的，是物理学的克兰教授（Doctor Crane）。圣彼得与他来往颇多，虽然在专业之外这人很乏味——一个心胸狭窄、面目也令人不快到难堪地步的人。多年前克兰便开始染上一种病，到后来证明是不治之症，如今定期要把他送进医院动手术。在汤姆·奥特兰到来之前，圣彼得在汉密尔顿没有任何一个朋友能让莉莲嫉妒；汤姆天生如此、早年的环境又恰好适合，正好能在《北美的西班牙冒险家》的工作上助他一臂之力。

===Original===
When he had almost reached his old house and his study, the Professor remembered that he really must have an understanding with his landlord, or the place would be rented over his head. He turned and went down into another part of the city, by the car shops, where only workmen lived, and found his landlord’s little toy house, set on a hillside, over a basement faced up with red brick and covered with hop vines. Old Appelhoff was sitting on a bench before his door, making a broom. Raising broom corn was one of his economies. Beside him was his dachshund bitch, Minna.
St. Peter explained that he wanted to stay on in the empty house, and would pay the full rent each month. So irregular a project annoyed Appelhoff. “I like fine to oblige you, Professor, but dey is several parties looking at de house already, an’ I don’t like to lose a year’s rent for maybe a few months.”
“Oh, that’s all right, Fred. I’ll take it for the year, to simplify matters. I want to finish my new book before I move.”
Fred still looked uneasy. “I better see de insurance man, eh? It says for purposes of domestic dwelling.”
“He won’t object. Let’s have a look at your garden. What a fine crop of apples and sickle pears you have!”
“I don’t like dem trees what don’t bear not’ing,” said the old man with sly humour, remembering the Professor’s glistening, barren shrubs and the good ground wasted behind his stucco wall.
“How about your linden-trees?”
“Oh, dem flowers is awful good for de headache!”
“You don’t look as if you were subject to it, Fred.”
“Not me, but my woman always had.”
“Pretty lonesome without her, Applehoff?”
“I miss her, Professor, but I ain’t just lonesome.” The old man rubbed his bristly chin. “My Minna here is most like a person, and den I got so many t’ings to t’ink about.”
“Have you? Pleasant things, I hope?”
“Well, all kinds. When I was young, in de old country, I had it hard to git my wife at all, an’ I never had time to t’ink. When I come to dis country I had to work so terrible hard on dat farm to make crops an’ pay debts, dat I was like a horse. Now I have it easy, an’ I take time to t’ink about all dem t’ings.”
St. Peter laughed. “We all come to it, Applehoff. That’s one thing I’m renting your house for, to have room to think. Good morning.”

===Chinese===
眼看快到他的旧屋和他的书房了，教授才想起，他实在该和房东把话说清楚，不然这处房子会被人从他头上租走。他转身下了坡，往城市的另一片去，到那只有工人居住的、靠着铁路机车厂的街区，寻见了他房东那栋小小的玩具似的房子，坐落在山坡上，下面有一层地下室，外墙贴着红砖，覆满了蛇麻藤。老阿佩尔霍夫（Appelhoff）正坐在门前的长凳上扎扫帚。种帚高粱自家用，是他勤俭持家的法子之一。他身旁卧着他的那条母腊肠犬，明娜（Minna）。
圣彼得说明他想在那空房子里继续住下去，每月照付全额房租。这样不合常情的要求惹得阿佩尔霍夫不大乐意。「我很乐意帮你的忙，教授，可已经有好几拨人来看过这房子啦，我可不想为了也许就那么几个月，白白丢了一年的房租。」
「哦，这没关系，弗雷德（Fred）。我把它包下一年就是了，省得啰嗦。我想在搬之前写完我的新书。」
弗雷德还是面有难色。「我最好还是去问问保险公司的伙计，嗯？那上头写着只供居住之用。」
「他不会反对的。让我看看你的园子。你这一茬苹果和镰刀梨可真好！」
「我可不稀罕那种什么都不结的树，」老头带着狡黠的幽默说，一边想起教授那些亮闪闪却光开花不结果的花木，还有他那灰泥墙后白白糟蹋掉的好地。
「那你那些椴树呢？」
「哦，那花对治头痛可顶用啦！」
「看你这模样，可不像是犯头痛的人，弗雷德。」
「我是没事，可我那口子从前老犯。」
「她不在了，挺孤单吧，阿佩尔霍夫？」
「我想她，教授，可我倒也不算怎么孤单。」老头搓了搓他毛茬茬的下巴。「我这里明娜差不多就像个人似的，再说我还有那么多事情要想呢。」
「是么？但愿都是些叫人高兴的事吧？」
「嗐，什么事都有。我年轻那会儿在老家，娶个老婆都难极了，哪有工夫想。等到了这国家，在那农庄上为收成、为还债干那么要命的苦活，我简直像一匹马。如今我日子好过了，我就腾出工夫，把这些事统统想一遍。」
圣彼得笑了。「我们大家最终都走到这一步，阿佩尔霍夫。我租你这房子，有一半也是图个有地方好想事。早安。」

===Original===
Crossing the public park, on his way back to the old house, he espied his professional rival and enemy, Professor Horace Langtry, taking a Sunday morning stroll—very well got up in English clothes he had brought back from his customary summer in London, with a bowler hat of unusual block and a horn-handled walking-stick. In twenty years the two men had scarcely had speech with each other beyond a stiff “good morning.” When Langtry first came to the university he looked hardly more than a boy, with curly brown hair and such a fresh complexion that the students called him Lily Langtry. His round pink cheeks and round eyes and round chin made him look rather like a baby grown big. All these years had made little difference, except that his curls were now quite grey, his rosy cheeks even rosier, and his mouth dropped a little at the corners, so that he looked like a baby suddenly grown old and rather cross about it.
Seeing St. Peter, the younger man turned abruptly into a side alley, but the Professor overtook him.
“Good morning, Langtry. These elms are becoming real trees at last. They’ve changed a good deal since we first came here.”
Doctor Langtry moved his rosy chin sidewise over his high double collar. “Good morning, Doctor St. Peter. I really don’t remember much about the trees. They seem to be doing well now.”
St. Peter stepped abreast of him. “There have been many changes, Langtry, and not all of them are good. Don’t you notice a great difference in the student body as a whole, in the new crop that comes along every year now—how different they are from the ones of our early years here?”
The smooth chin turned again, and the other professor of European history blinked. “In just what respect?”
“Oh, in the all-embracing respect of quality! We have hosts of students, but they’re a common sort.”
“Perhaps. I can’t say I’ve noticed it.” The air between the two colleagues was not thawing out any. A church-bell rang. Langtry started hopefully. “You must excuse me, Doctor St. Peter, I am on my way to service.”
The Professor gave it up with a shrug. “All right, all right, Langtry, as you will. *Quelle folie!*”
Langtry half turned back, hesitated on the ball of his suddenly speeding foot, and said with faultless politeness: “I beg your pardon?”
St. Peter waved his hand with a gesture of negation, and detained the churchgoer no longer. He sauntered along slackly through the hot September sunshine, wondering why Langtry didn’t see the absurdity of their long grudge. They had always been directly opposed in matters of university policy, until it had almost become a part of their professional duties to outwit and cramp each other.

===Chinese===
穿过公共公园回旧屋的路上，他远远瞥见他的同行冤家和对头——霍勒斯·朗特里教授（Professor Horace Langtry）——正在做星期日早晨的散步。他穿戴得颇为讲究，是一身从惯常度夏的伦敦带回来的英式衣装，戴着一顶式样别致的圆顶硬礼帽，拄着一根角柄手杖。二十年来，这两个人除了客客气气的一句「早安」，几乎没有说过别的话。朗特里初到这所大学时，看上去几乎还是个孩子，一头棕色鬈发，脸色又那么鲜嫩，学生们管他叫「莉莉·朗特里」（Lily Langtry）。他那圆圆的粉腮、圆圆的眼睛、圆圆的下巴，叫人觉得他活像一个长成大人模样的婴孩。这些年在脸上几乎没留下什么痕迹，只是鬈发如今已相当花白，粉腮更红了些，嘴角微微往下耷拉了一点——于是他看上去像一个忽然变老了的婴孩，而且对此颇为恼怒。
一看见圣彼得，这年轻人便猛地拐进一条小巷，可教授已经追了上去。
「早安，朗特里。这些榆树总算长得像棵树了。自从我们头一回来这儿，它们可是变了不少。」
朗特里博士把他那粉色的下巴在高高的硬翻领上往旁边一转。「早安，圣彼得博士。那些树，我还真记不大清了。如今它们长得好像还不错。」
圣彼得走到与他并齐。「变化是很多的，朗特里，而且并非全都变好了。你难道没注意到，整个学生群体、每年涌进来这一茬新的，跟当年我们刚来时的那些学生，有多大的不同么？」
那光洁的下巴又转了过来，这另一位欧洲史教授眨了眨眼。「在哪一方面呢？」
「哦，在无所不包的——品质那一方面！我们的学生成群结队，可都是些平庸之辈。」
「也许吧。我倒没怎么留意。」两位同事之间的空气丝毫没有回暖。一声教堂的钟响。朗特里满怀希望地动了动。「失陪了，圣彼得博士，我正要去礼拜。」
教授耸了耸肩，作罢。「好吧，好吧，朗特里，随你。多么荒唐啊（*Quelle folie*）！」
朗特里半转过身，在那突然加快的脚尖上犹豫了一下，以无可挑剔的礼貌说道：「您说什么？」
圣彼得摆了摆手，做个否定的姿势，不再挽留这位去做礼拜的人了。他在九月的烈日下懒洋洋地闲荡着，心里纳闷朗特里怎么就看不出他们这场宿怨的荒唐。在大学的种种事务上，他们向来针锋相对，到后来，互相算计、互相掣肘几乎成了他们各自职务分内的一部分。

===Original===
When young Langtry first came there, his specialty was supposed to be American history. His uncle was president of the board of regents, and very influential in State politics; the institution had to look to him, indeed, to get its financial appropriations passed by the Legislature. Langtry was a Tory in his point of view, and was considered very English in his tone and manner. His lectures were dull, and the students didn’t like him. Every inducement was offered to make his courses popular. Liberal credits were given for collateral reading. A student could read almost anything that had ever been written in the United States and get credit for it in American history. He could charge up the time spent in perusing *The Scarlet Letter* to Colonial history, and *Tom Sawyer* to the Missouri Compromise, it was said. St. Peter openly criticized these lax methods, both to the faculty and to the regents. Naturally, “Madame Langtry” paid him out. During the Professor’s second Sabbatical year in Spain, Horace and his uncle together very nearly got his department away from him. They worked so quietly that it was only at the eleventh hour that St. Peter’s old students throughout the State got wind of what was going on, dropped their various businesses and professions for a few days, and came up to the capital in dozens and saved his place for him. The opposition had been so formidable that when it came time for his third year away, the Professor had not dared ask for it, but had taken an extension of his summer vacation instead. The fact that he was carrying on another line of work than his lectures, and was publishing books that weren’t strictly textbooks, had been used against him by Langtry’s uncle.

===Chinese===
小朗特里初来乍到时，他的专长据说还是美国史。他伯父是评议会主席，在本州政坛颇有势力；学校要使自己的经费拨款在州议会通过，的确还得仰仗他。朗特里的立场是个托利党人，谈吐举止都被认为非常英国派。他的讲课乏味得很，学生们都不喜欢他。校方千方百计使他的课变得受欢迎。课外阅读被给予宽厚的学分。一个学生几乎可以读遍美国有史以来写过的一切，把它算作美国史的学分。据说，他可以把啃《红字》（*The Scarlet Letter*）的时间记到殖民史的账上，把《汤姆·索亚历险记》（*Tom Sawyer*）记到密苏里妥协案的账上。圣彼得曾当着全体教员的面、也当着评议员的面，公开批评过这些松松垮垮的做法。自然，这位「朗特里夫人」是要报复的。教授第二次到西班牙休学那年，霍勒斯和他伯父两人差点把他的系从他手里夺走。他们干得悄无声息，直到最后一刻，圣彼得遍布全州的老学生们才嗅出风声，纷纷丢下各自的生意和营生几天，成打地赶到州首府来，才替他保住了位子。那次反对的声势之猛烈，到了他第三个该休学年的当口，教授竟不敢再开口申请，只好改请一段延长的暑假来代替。他除了讲义之外还在从事另一条线的工作、所出版的书又不严格算是教科书，这一事实也被朗特里的伯父拿来当作反对他的把柄。

===Original===
As Langtry felt that the unpopularity of his course was due to his subject, a new chair was created for him. There couldn’t be two heads in European history, so the board of regents made for him a chair of Renaissance history, or, as St. Peter said, a Renaissance chair of history. Of late years, for reasons that had not much to do with his lectures, Langtry had prospered better. To the new generations of country and village boys now pouring into the university in such large numbers, Langtry had become, in a curious way, an instructor in manners—what is called an “influence.” To the football-playing farmer boy who had a good allowance but didn’t know how to dress or what to say, Langtry looked like a shortcut. He had several times taken parties of undergraduates to London for the summer, and they had come back wonderfully brushed up. He introduced a very popular fraternity into the university, and its members looked after his interests, as did its affiliated sorority. His standing on the faculty was now quite as good as St. Peter’s own, and the Professor wondered what Langtry still had to be sore about.
What was the use of keeping up the feud? They had both come there young men, fighting for their places and their lives; now they were not very young any more; they would neither of them, probably, ever hold a better position. Couldn’t Langtry see it was a draw, that they had both been beaten?

===Chinese===
朗特里觉得自己开的课不受欢迎，要怪只能怪他所教的那门学科，于是校方又为他特设了一个新讲席。欧洲史不能有两个主任，评议会便为他设了一个文艺复兴史的讲席——或者如圣彼得所说，一个文艺复兴式的史学讲席。近些年来，由于一些与他讲课无关的原因，朗特里的光景好多了。对如今正成批涌进大学的新一代乡下、村镇男孩而言，朗特里以一种古怪的方式成了一个礼仪课老师——也就是所谓的一种「影响」。对一个手头宽裕、却既不懂穿戴、又不知说什么的橄榄球农场男孩来说，朗特里看上去像一条捷径。他曾好几回带一群本科生到伦敦去过暑假，他们回来时一个个都被修整得焕然一新。他把一个极受欢迎的兄弟会引入了大学，其成员处处照应着他的利益，与之有联系的姐妹会也一样。他如今在系里的地位，已经和圣彼得不相上下，所以教授想不明白朗特里还有什么可怀恨的。
这宿怨还维持着做什么呢？他们都是年轻时来到此地的，为自己的位子、为自己的生计搏斗；如今两人都不算很年轻了；他们俩，多半谁也坐不上一个更好的位子了。朗特里难道就看不见，这是一场不分胜负的局，他们俩都被打败了么？

### IV / IV

===Original===
On Monday afternoon St. Peter mounted to his study and lay down on the box-couch, tired out with his day at the university. The first few weeks of the year were very fatiguing for him; there were so many exhausting things besides his lectures and all the new students; long faculty meetings in which almost no one was ever frank, and always the old fight to keep up the standard of scholarship, to prevent the younger professors, who had a sharp eye to their own interests, from farming the whole institution out to athletics, and to the agricultural and commercial schools favoured and fostered by the State Legislature.
The September heat, too, was hard on him. He wanted to be out at the lake every day—it was never so fine as in late September. He was lying with closed eyes, resting his mind on the picture of intense autumn-blue water, when he heard a tap at the door and his daughter Rosamond entered, very handsome in a silk suit of a vivid shade of lilac, admirably suited to her complexion and showing that in the colour of her cheeks there was actually a tone of warm lavender. In that low room she seemed very tall indeed, a little out of drawing, as, to her father's eye, she so often did. Usually, however, people were aware only of her rich complexion, her curving, unresisting mouth and mysterious eyes. Tom Outland had seen nothing else, and he was a young man who saw a great deal.

===Chinese===
星期一下午，圣彼得上了他的书房，在箱式长榻上躺下，在大学里忙了一整天，他已经筋疲力尽了。每个学年的头几周对他来说总是非常累人；除了他自己的课和一应新生之外，还有许多叫人疲惫的事：冗长的教职员会议，会上几乎谁也不肯说实话；以及那场永不停歇的旧仗——维护学术的水准，防着那些处处为自己打算的年轻教授，别让他们把整所学校都拱手交给体育，交给由州议会偏私扶持的农业学校和商业学校。
九月的暑热也叫他吃不消。他每天都想往湖上去——九月下旬的湖，是一年中再好不过的时候了。他闭着眼躺着，心里浮现出那片浓烈的秋日蓝水的图景，正歇息着，忽然听见一阵叩门声，跟着他女儿罗莎蒙德（Rosamond）走了进来。她穿着一身鲜亮的紫丁香色丝绸套裙，煞是好看，那颜色正配她的肤色，又显出她双颊的颜色里，的确带一抹暖融融的淡紫。在这间低矮的屋子里，她显得格外高挑，略微有些失了比例——在父亲眼里，她常常是这般模样。可旁人通常只注意到她那丰润的肤色、那弯弯的、毫无抗拒之意的嘴，和那双神秘的眼睛。汤姆·奥特兰当年眼里也再没有别的，而他是个见多识广的年轻人。

===Original===
"Am I interrupting something important, Papa?"
"No, not at all, my dear. Sit down."
On his writing-table she caught a glimpse of pages in a handwriting not his—a script she knew very well.
"Not much choice of chairs, is there?" she smiled. "Papa, I don't like to have you working in a place like this. It's not fitting."
"Much easier than to break in a new room, Rosie. A workroom should be like an old shoe; no matter how shabby, it's better than a new one."
"That's really what I came to see you about." Rosamond traced the edge of a hole in the matting with the tip of her lilac sunshade. "Won't you let me build you a little study in the back yard of the new house? I have such good ideas for it, and you would have no bother about it at all."
"Oh, thank you, Rosamond. It's most awfully nice of you to think of it. But keep it just an idea—it's better so. Lots of things are. For the present I'll plod on here. It's absurd, but it suits me. Habit is such a big part of work."

===Chinese===
「我打断了什么要紧的事吗，爸爸？」
「没有，一点也没有，亲爱的。坐下吧。」
他的写字台上，她瞥见了几页字迹，并非他手笔——那笔迹她再熟悉不过。
「这儿的椅子可没什么可挑的，是吧？」她笑了笑，「爸爸，我不喜欢让您在这样一个地方工作。不大相宜。」
「比新开辟一间屋子可容易多了，罗西。一间工作室就该像一只旧鞋；再怎么破旧，也比新的强。」
「我正是为这事来见您的。」罗莎蒙德用她那把紫丁香色阳伞的伞尖，沿着席子上一处破洞的边缘描着。「您就让我在新宅的后院给您盖一间小书房，好不好？我心里有好多好主意，您一点儿都不必操心。」
「哦，谢谢你，罗莎蒙德。你能想着这个，真是太好了。可还是让它只留作一个想法吧——这样更好。许多事都是这样。眼下我还要在这儿凑合一阵子。这地方是可笑，可它合我的意。习惯，原是工作里的一大半呢。」

===Original===
"With Augusta's old things lying about, and those dusty old forms? Why didn't she at least get those out of your way?"
"Oh, they have a right here, by long tenure. It's their room, too. I don't want to come upon them lying in some dump-heap on the road to the lake. They remind me of the times when you were little girls, and your first party frocks used to hang on them at night, when I worked."
Rosamond smiled, unconvinced. "Papa, don't joke with me. I've come to talk about something serious, and it's very difficult. You know I'm a little afraid of you." She dropped her shadowy, bewitching eyes.
"Afraid of me? Never!"
"Oh, yes, I am when you're sarcastic. You musn't be today, please. Louie and I have often talked this over. We feel strongly about it. He's often been on the point of blurting out with it, but I've curbed him. You don't always approve of Louie and me.
