第二天早上，棚子里我那匹坐骑的嘶鸣声把我惊醒了。我把它牵到小径底下我搁箱子的地方，把我的物件驮在它背上，运上小屋。那天夜里我坐到很晚，等布雷克回来，虽然明知他不会来。过了几天，我骑马去塔平打听他的消息。比尔·胡克让我看了罗迪那匹马。他已经以六十块钱的价钱把它卖给了马房。站长告诉我，布雷克买了一张去亚利桑那州温斯洛（Winslow）的车票。我给温斯洛的站长和调度员都拍了电报，可他们都给不出什么消息。迪谢纳神父巡游路过，我把事情原原本本告诉了他。

===Original===
He thought Blake would come back sometime, that I’d only miss him if I went out to look for him. He advised me to stay on the mesa that summer and get ahead with my studies, work up my Spanish grammar and my Latin. He had friends all along the Santa Fe, and he was sure we could catch Blake by advertising in the local papers along the road; Albuquerque, Winslow, Flagstaff, Williams, Los Angeles. After a few days with him, I went back to the mesa to wait.

===Chinese===
他觉得布雷克迟早会回来，我要是出去找他，反倒会错过他。他劝我那年夏天就留在台地上，把功课赶一赶，把西班牙语语法和拉丁文好好做一做。他在圣菲铁路沿线到处都有朋友，他肯定我们只要在那条路沿线的地方报纸上登广告，就一定能逮到布雷克；阿尔伯克基、温斯洛、弗拉格斯塔夫、威廉斯、洛杉矶。我在他那儿住了几天，就回台地上等着。

===Original===
I’ll never forget the night I got back. I crossed the river an hour before sunset and hobbled my horse in the wide bottom of Cow Canyon. The moon was up, though the sun hadn’t set, and it had that glittering silveriness the early stars have in high altitudes. The heavenly bodies look so much more remote from the bottom of a deep canyon than they do from the level. The climb of the walls helps out the eye, somehow. I lay down on a solitary rock that was like an island in the bottom of the valley, and looked up. The grey sagebrush and the blue-grey rock around me were already in shadow, but high above me the canyon walls were dyed flame-colour with the sunset, and the Cliff City lay in a gold haze against its dark cavern. In a few minutes it, too, was grey, and only the rim rock at the top held the red light. When that was gone, I could still see the copper glow in the piñons along the edge of the top ledges. The arc of sky over the canyon was silvery blue, with its pale yellow moon, and presently stars shivered into it, like crystals dropped into perfectly clear water.

===Chinese===
我永远忘不了回去那晚的情景。我在日落前一个钟头过了河，把马绊在牛峡那宽阔的谷底里。月亮已经升起来了，尽管太阳还没落，它带着一种高海拔处初升星辰才有的那种闪闪发亮的银辉。从一道深峡谷的谷底望上去，那些天体比从平地上望要显得遥远得多。峡谷两侧峭壁的节节攀升，不知怎的，倒帮了眼睛的忙。我在一块孤零零的岩石上躺下来，那岩石好比谷底的一座小岛，我仰面望着。我周围灰色的山艾丛和蓝灰色的岩石已经笼在阴影里了，可是在我头顶高处，峡谷的石壁还染着落日的火红色，而崖城笼在一层金黄的薄霭里，衬着它背后幽暗的岩洞。过了几分钟，连这也变成了灰色，只有崖顶的檐岩还燃着红光。等那红光也熄了，我还能看见崖顶边缘那些矮松里泛着的铜色微光。峡谷上空那一弯天穹是银蓝色的，挂着一轮淡黄的月亮，不一会儿，星辰便纷纷抖落进天幕，就像水晶投进一澄到底的清水里那般。

===Original===
I remember these things, because, in a sense, that was the first night I was ever really on the mesa at all—the first night that all of me was there. This was the first time I ever saw it as a whole. It all came together in my understanding, as a series of experiments do when you begin to see where they are leading. Something had happened in me that made it possible for me to coordinate and simplify, and that process, going on in my mind, brought with it great happiness. It was possession. The excitement of my first discovery was a very pale feeling compared to this one. For me the mesa was no longer an adventure, but a religious emotion. I had read of filial piety in the Latin poets, and I knew that was what I felt for this place. It had formerly been mixed up with other motives; but now that they were gone, I had my happiness unalloyed.

===Chinese===
我记得这些事，因为从某种意义上说，那才是我头一回真真正正待在台地上——头一回我整个的人都到了那儿。这也是我头一回把它当作一个整体来看。这一切在我心中渐渐归拢到了一起，就像一连串实验，等你开始看清它们要引向何处时那样。我内心里起了某种变化，使我得以梳理、提纯，而这个过程在我心头进行着，随之带来了莫大的幸福。那是一种占有。我初次发现它时的那份激动，和这一刻比起来，实在是一种很淡薄的感觉。对我来说，台地不再是一场冒险，而成了一种宗教般的情感。我在拉丁诗人的作品里读到过孝敬这种情感，我知道那正是我对这个地方所怀的心情。从前这种情感还掺杂着别的动机；可如今那些都消失了，我便有了我那毫无掺杂的幸福。

===Original===
What that night began lasted all summer. I stayed on the mesa until November. It was the first time I’d ever studied methodically, or intelligently. I got the better of the Spanish grammar and read the twelve books of the *Aeneid*. I studied in the morning, and in the afternoon I worked at clearing away the mess the German had made in packing—tidying up the ruins to wait another hundred years, maybe, for the right explorer. I can scarcely hope that life will give me another summer like that one. It was my high tide. Every morning, when the sun’s rays first hit the mesa top, while the rest of the world was in shadow, I wakened with the feeling that I had found everything, instead of having lost everything. Nothing tired me. Up there alone, a close neighbour to the sun, I seemed to get the solar energy in some direct way. And at night, when I watched it drop down behind the edge of the plain below me, I used to feel that I couldn’t have borne another hour of that consuming light, that I was full to the brim, and needed dark and sleep.

===Chinese===
那一夜所开启的东西，持续了整整一个夏天。我在台地上一直待到十一月。这是我头一回有条理地、用心地读书。我拿下了西班牙语语法，读完了十二卷《埃涅阿斯纪》。我上午读书，下午就去清理那个德国人打包时弄出的那堆乱摊子——把废墟收拾停当，好让它们也许再等上一百年，等来真正配得上它们的探险家。我几乎不敢指望此生还能再有一个那样的夏天。那是我生命里的高潮。每天清晨，当太阳的光芒头一次落到台地顶上，而世上其余的一切还笼罩在阴影里时，我醒来，只觉得我已经寻得了一切，而不是失去了一切。没有什么能叫我疲倦。独自在那高旷之地，与太阳比邻而居，我仿佛以一种直截了当的方式汲取着太阳的能量。到了夜里，当我望着它沉入我脚下平原的边缘之下时，我常会觉得自己再也承受不住那耗人的光芒再多一个钟头，觉得自己已经满溢到唇边，需要黑暗与睡眠。

===Original===
All that summer, I never went up to the Eagle’s Nest to get my diary—indeed, it’s probably there yet. I didn’t feel the need of that record. It would have been going backward. I didn’t want to go back and unravel things step by step. Perhaps I was afraid that I would lose the whole in the parts. At any rate, I didn’t go for my record.

===Chinese===
整整那一夏，我都没上鹰巢去取我的日记——说真的，那本子多半至今还在那儿呢。我觉得不需要那份记录。那只会是一种倒退。我不想回过头去，把事情一桩一桩地再拆解开。也许我是怕自己会在那些细枝末节里失掉了整体。无论如何，我没去取我的记录。

===Original===
During those months I didn’t worry much about poor Roddy. I told myself the advertisements would surely get him—I knew his habit of reading newspapers. There are times when one’s vitality is too high to be clouded, too elastic to stay down. Hurrying from my cabin in the morning to the spot in the Cliff City where I studied under a cedar, I used to be frightened at my own heartlessness. But the feel of the narrow moccasin-worn trail in the flat rock made my feet glad, like a good taste in the mouth, and I’d forget all about Blake without knowing it. I found I was reading too fast; so I began to commit long passages of Virgil to memory—if it hadn’t been for that, I might have forgotten how to use my voice, or gone to talking to myself. When I look into the *Aeneid* now, I can always see two pictures: the one on the page, and another behind that: blue and purple rocks and yellow-green piñons with flat tops, little clustered houses clinging together for protection, a rude tower rising in their midst, rising strong, with calmness and courage—behind it a dark grotto, in its depths a crystal spring.

===Chinese===
那几个月里，我并没有怎么为可怜的罗迪发愁。我对自己说，那些广告一定能把他找回来——我知道他爱看报纸。一个人生命的元气有时高扬得不容任何阴霾笼罩，太有弹性，压不下去。每天早上我从小屋匆匆赶到崖城里我读书的那棵雪松底下时，我常常会被自己的没心没肺吓一跳。可那平整岩石上被鹿皮鞋踩出来的窄径，踩在脚底有一种叫人欢喜的感觉，就像嘴里尝到的一味好滋味，我便不知不觉地把布雷克忘得一干二净。我发现自己读得太快了；于是我开始大段大段地背诵维吉尔——要不是那样，我或许会忘了怎样使用自己的嗓音，或者干脆自言自语起来。如今我再翻开《埃涅阿斯纪》，总能看见两幅图景：一幅在书页上，另一幅在那背后：蓝紫色的岩石，黄绿色的平顶矮松，一簇簇紧紧挤在一起以求庇护的小屋，一座粗朴的塔楼矗立其间，坚强地矗立着，带着沉静与勇气——塔楼背后是一个幽暗的石洞，洞的最深处有一眼水晶般的泉水。

===Original===
Happiness is something one can’t explain. You must take my word for it. Troubles enough came afterward, but there was that summer, high and blue, a life in itself.

===Chinese===
幸福这东西是没法子解释的。你只能信我的话。后来的烦恼够多的了，可有那样一个夏天，高旷、湛蓝，本身就足以抵得上一生。

===Original===
Next winter I went back to Pardee and stayed with the O’Briens again, working on the section and studying with Father Duchene and trying to get some word of Blake. Now that I was back on the railroad, I thought I couldn’t fail to find him. I went out to Winslow and to Williams, and I questioned the railroad men. We advertised for him in every possible way, had all the Santa Fe operatives and the police and the Catholic missionaries on the watch for him, offered a thousand dollars reward for whoever found him. But it came to nothing. Father Duchene and our friends down there are still looking. But the older I grow, the more I understand what it was I did that night on the mesa. Anyone who requites faith and friendship as I did, will have to pay for it. I’m not very sanguine about good fortune for myself. I’ll be called to account when I least expect it.

===Chinese===
第二年冬天，我回到帕迪，又住在奥布赖恩家，在工段上做工，跟迪谢纳神父读书，还想方设法打听布雷克的下落。既然我又回到了铁路上，我想我总不至于找不到他。我去了温斯洛，去了威廉斯，向那些铁路上的人打听。我们想尽了一切办法登广告找他，把圣菲铁路沿线所有的员工、警察，还有天主教传教士都动员起来留意他，还悬赏一千块钱给找到他的人。可这一切都落了空。迪谢纳神父和我们南边的朋友至今还在找。只是我年纪越长，就越明白那天夜里我在台地上所做的事究竟意味着什么。谁要是像我那样辜负了信赖与情谊，总得为此付出代价。我对自己的福分并不怎么乐观。迟早有一天，会在我最意想不到的时候，被找去清账的。

===Original===
In the spring, just a year after I quarrelled with Roddy, I landed here and walked into your garden, and the rest you know.

===Chinese===
到了春天，就在我和罗迪闹翻的整整一年之后，我到了这儿，走进了你家的花园，往后的事你都知道了。

### I / I

===Original===
All the most important things in his life, St. Peter sometimes reflected, had been determined by chance. His education in France had been an accident. His married life had been happy largely through a circumstance with which neither he nor his wife had anything to do. They had been young people with good qualities, and very much in love, but they could not have been happy if Lillian had not inherited a small income from her father—only about sixteen hundred a year, but it had made all the difference in the world. A few memorable interregnums between servants had let him know that Lillian couldn’t pinch and be shabby and do housework, as the wives of some of his colleagues did. Under such conditions she became another person, and a bitter one.

===Chinese===
圣彼得有时想，他一生中所有最要紧的事，其实都是偶然决定的。他在法国所受的教育，纯属一桩意外。他的婚姻生活之所以幸福，在很大程度上也要归功于一件与他夫妻二人全不相干的情由。他们当年都是品行很好的年轻人，又深深地相爱，可要是莉莲没有从父亲那儿继承下一笔小小的进项——一年不过一千六百来块，那情形就会截然不同。他经历过几段两任佣人之间令人难忘的空档，由此深知莉莲做不到像他某些同事的太太那样精打细算、寒酸度日、亲手操持家务。处在那样的境地里，她就会变成另一个人，一个尖酸刻薄的人。

===Original===
Tom Outland had been a stroke of chance he couldn’t possibly have imagined; his strange coming, his strange story, his devotion, his early death and posthumous fame—it was all fantastic. Fantastic, too, that this tramp boy should amass a fortune for someone whose name he had never heard, for “an extravagant and wheeling stranger.” The Professor often thought of that curiously bitter burst from the baritone in Brahms’ *Requiem*, attending the words, “*He heapeth up riches and cannot tell who shall scatter them!*” The vehemence of this passage had seemed to him uncalled for until he read it by the light of the history of his own family.

===Chinese===
汤姆·奥特兰的出现，是一道他做梦也想不到的机缘；他奇特的到来，他奇特的故事，他的赤诚，他的英年早逝和身后的声名——这一切都离奇得很。同样离奇的还有：这个流浪少年竟会为一个他从未听过的姓氏、为「一个奢靡而油滑的陌生人」积下一笔家财。教授常常想起勃拉姆斯《德意志安魂曲》里男中音那段出奇愤激的乐句，唱的正是那些字眼：「他堆积财富，却不知将由谁来驱散！」从前他总觉得这一段的凌厉来得没来由，直到他借着自己一家人的遭际去读它，才明白过来。

===Original===
St. Peter thought he had fared well with fate. He wouldn’t choose to live his life over—he might not have such good luck again. He had had two romances: one of the heart, which had filled his life for many years, and a second of the mind—of the imagination. Just when the morning brightness of the world was wearing off for him, along came Outland and brought him a kind of second youth.

===Chinese===
圣彼得觉得自己在命运手里算是得了便宜。他不愿把这一生再重过一遍——未必还能再交这样好的运。他有过两段罗曼史：一段属于心，许多年间充盈了他的整个生活；第二段属于心智——属于想象。正当这世间的晨光朝晖在他身上渐渐褪色之际，奥特兰来了，给他带来了某种意义上的第二次青春。

===Original===
Through Outland’s studies, long after they had ceased to be pupil and master, he had been able to experience afresh things that had grown dull with use. The boy’s mind had the superabundance of heat which is always present where there is rich germination. To share his thoughts was to see old perspectives transformed by new effects of light.

===Chinese===
在奥特兰做学问的过程中，早在他俩已不再是师生之后许久，他仍能借以重新体味那些因日久用熟而变得寡淡的事物。这少年的心智里有一种丰沛过盛的热力，凡是生机丰茂之地，总有这种热力氤氲其间。与他共享所思所想，便如同看见旧的景致被新的光效所点化。

===Original===
If the last four volumes of *The Spanish Adventurers* were more simple and inevitable than those that went before, it was largely because of Outland. When St. Peter first began his work, he realized that his great drawback was the lack of early association, the fact that he had not spent his youth in the great dazzling Southwest country which was the scene of his explorers’ adventures. By the time he had got as far as the third volume, into his house walked a boy who had grown up there, a boy with imagination, with the training and insight resulting from a very curious experience; who had in his pocket the secrets which old trails and stones and watercourses tell only to adolescence.

===Chinese===
《北美的西班牙冒险家》最后四卷之所以比前头几卷更为单纯、更带一种水到渠成的味道，在很大程度上要归功于奥特兰。圣彼得刚开始动笔这部书时，就明白自己最大的短板是缺少早年的耳濡目染——他没能把青春岁月消磨在那片叫人眼花缭乱的西南部土地上，而那里正是他所写的那些探险家历险的舞台。等他写到第三卷，一个少年走进了他的家门，一个在那片土地上长大的少年，一个富有想象力、凭着一段极不寻常的经历磨砺出训练与眼力的少年；他衣袋里揣着的，是那些古道、石头与水道只肯对青春期吐露的秘密。

===Original===
Two years after Tom’s graduation they took the copy of Fray Garces’ manuscript that the Professor had made from the original in Spain, and went down into the Southwest together. By autumn they had been over every mile of his trail on horseback. Tom could take a sentence from Garces’ diary and find the exact spot at which the missionary crossed the Rio Colorado on a certain Sunday in 1775. Given one pueblo, he could always find the route by which the priest had reached the next.

===Chinese===
汤姆毕业两年之后，他们带着教授从西班牙的原稿抄录回来的加尔塞斯神父那份手稿的副本，一同南下深入西南部。到了秋天，他们已经骑着马把那位传教士走过的每一英里路都踏了一遍。汤姆能从加尔塞斯的日记里拈出一句话，就找到一七七五年某个礼拜天那位传教士渡过科罗拉多河的确切地点。只要给他一个普韦布洛村落，他总能找出那位神父是从哪条路走到下一个村落的。

===Original===
It was on that trip that they went to Tom’s Blue Mesa, climbed the ladder of spliced pine-trees to the Cliff City, and up to the Eagle’s Nest. There they took Tom’s diary from the stone cupboard where he had sealed it up years ago, before he set out for Washington on his fruitless errand.

===Chinese===
正是在那次旅行中，他们去了汤姆的蓝色台地，爬上那架用松树拼接成的梯子登上崖城，又登上鹰巢。在那儿，他们从汤姆多年前动身去华盛顿办那趟徒劳的差事之前封存起来的石橱里，取出了他的日记。

===Original===
The next summer Tom went with the Professor to Old Mexico. They had planned a third summer together, in Paris, but it never came off. Outland was delayed by the formalities of securing his patent, and then came August, 1914. Father Duchene, the missionary priest who had been Tom’s teacher, stopped in Hamilton on his way back to Belgium, hurrying home to serve in any capacity he might. The rugged old man stayed in Hamilton only four days, but in that time Outland made up his mind, had a will drawn, packed, and said goodbye. He sailed with Father Duchene on the *Rochambeau*.

===Chinese===
第二年夏天，汤姆随教授去了墨西哥。他们原计划第三个夏天一起去巴黎，可这事终未实现。奥特兰为办妥专利的种种手续耽搁下来，接着便到了一九一四年八月。曾经做过汤姆老师的传教士迪谢纳神父，在赶回比利时的途中于汉密尔顿停留，他急着回国去尽自己所能效力。这位硬朗的老人在汉密尔顿只待了四天，可就在这几天里，奥特兰拿定了主意，立好遗嘱，收拾行装，一一告别。他随迪谢纳神父一道登上了「罗尚博号」。

===Original===
To this day St. Peter regretted that he had never got that vacation in Paris with Tom Outland. He had wanted to revisit certain spots with him: to go with him some autumn morning to the Luxembourg Gardens, when the yellow horse-chestnuts were bright and bitter after rain; to stand with him before the monument to Delacroix and watch the sun gleam on the bronze figures—Time, bearing away the youth who was struggling to snatch his palm—or was it to lay a palm? Not that it mattered. It might have mattered to Tom, had not chance, in one great catastrophe, swept away all youth and all palms, and almost Time itself.

===Chinese===
直到今天，圣彼得仍在为没能和汤姆·奥特兰同去巴黎度那次假而惋惜。他曾想和他重访几处旧地：想和他一道，在某个秋日的早晨去卢森堡公园，那时雨后的黄色七叶树明艳而苦涩；想和他一同站在德拉克洛瓦的纪念碑前，看阳光在那组铜像上闪耀——时光之神，正把那个挣扎着想攫取棕榈枝的少年挟走——又或者，他原是要献上一枝棕榈？这也无妨。可对于汤姆，这就未必无妨了——只因一场浩劫般的偶然，把所有的青春、所有的棕榈枝，几乎连时光本身，都一并席卷而去了。

---

===Original===
And suppose Tom had been more prudent, and had not gone away with his old teacher? St. Peter sometimes wondered what would have happened to him, once the trap of worldly success had been sprung on him. He couldn’t see Tom building “Outland,” or becoming a public-spirited citizen of Hamilton. What change would have come in his blue eye, in his fine long hand with the back-springing thumb, which had never handled things that were not the symbols of ideas? A hand like that, had he lived, must have been put to other uses. His fellow scientists, his wife, the town and State, would have required many duties of it. It would have had to write thousands of useless letters, frame thousands of false excuses. It would have had to “manage” a great deal of money, to be the instrument of a woman who would grow always more exacting. He had escaped all that. He had made something new in the world—and the rewards, the meaningless conventional gestures, he had left to others.

===Chinese===
再假如汤姆当年更审慎些，没有随他那位老教师远走呢？圣彼得有时也会想，一旦世俗功业那张罗网朝他当头罩下，他会变成什么样子。他想象不出汤姆会去营建「奥特兰」那座宅子，或是成为汉密尔顿城里一位热心公益的市民。他那只蓝眼睛，那只拇指往后翻翘的修长好看的手——那只手从未碰过任何不属于理念象征的物件——又会起怎样的变化？那样的一只手，要是他活着，势必得派作别的用场。他的科学界同人、他的妻子、这座城市和这个州，都会对它提出许许多多的差事。它将不得不写下成千上万封无用的信件，编造成千上万个虚伪的托词。它将不得不「打理」一大笔钱财，沦为一个欲求日甚的女子的工具。这一切他都逃脱了。他在这个世界上造出了全新的东西——至于那些酬报，那些毫无意义的俗套虚文，他都留给了别人。

### II / II

===Original===
All those summer days, while the Professor was sending cheerful accounts of his activities to his family in France, he was really doing very little. He had begun, in a desultory way, to annotate the diary that Tom had kept on the mesa, in which he had noted down the details of each day’s work among the ruins, along with the weather and anything unusual in the routine of their life. There was a minute description of each tool they found, of every piece of cloth and pottery, frequently accompanied by a very suggestive pencil sketch of the object and a surmise as to its use and the kind of life in which it had played a part. To St. Peter this plain account was almost beautiful, because of the stupidities it avoided and the things it did not say. If words had cost money, Tom couldn’t have used them more sparingly. The adjectives were purely descriptive, relating to form and colour, and were used to present the objects under consideration, not the young explorer’s emotions. Yet through this austerity one felt the kindling imagination, the ardour and excitement of the boy, like the vibration in a voice when the speaker strives to conceal his emotion by using only conventional phrases.

===Chinese===
在那整个夏天的日子里，教授一面给远在法国的家人寄去兴致勃勃的汇报，讲自己如何忙碌，实际上却几乎没做什么事。他有一搭没一搭地着手为汤姆在台地上记的那本日记作注解——那日记里逐日记着他们在废墟间每日工作的细节，连同天气如何、日常生活里有什么不寻常的事。每一件出土的工具、每一片布帛和陶器都有一份细密的描摹，往往还附上一幅极能引人联想的铅笔速写，并揣测它的用途、曾参与过怎样的生活。在圣彼得看来，这朴素的记述几乎是美的，美在它所避开的那些蠢话，美在它所没有说出口的那些话。倘若字字都要花钱，汤姆也不可能把它们用得更省了。那些形容词纯粹是描写性的，只关形与色，是为了呈现所考察的物件，而不是为了铺陈这位年轻探险家的情绪。然而透过这种克制，人仍能感到那少年被点燃的想象、他的热忱与兴奋，就像一个人竭力只用些套话来掩藏自己的情绪时，嗓音里仍会带出的那一丝颤动。

===Original===
When the first of August came round, the Professor realized that he had pleasantly trifled away nearly two months at a task which should have taken little more than a week. But he had been doing a good deal besides—something he had never before been able to do.

===Chinese===
等到了八月初，教授才发觉，自己竟悠哉游哉地把将近两个月的光阴消磨在一件本该只要一个多星期就能做完的事上。但除了这件事，他还做了不少别的——做了些他从前一直没法做到的事。

===Original===
St. Peter had always laughed at people who talked about “daydreams,” just as he laughed at people who naively confessed that they had “an imagination.” All his life his mind had behaved in a positive fashion. When he was not at work, or being actively amused, he went to sleep. He had no twilight stage. But now he enjoyed this half-awake loafing with his brain as if it were a new sense, arriving late, like wisdom teeth. He found he could lie on his sand-spit by the lake for hours and watch the seven motionless pines drink up the sun. In the evening, after dinner, he could sit idle and watch the stars, with the same immobility. He was cultivating a novel mental dissipation—and enjoying a new friendship. Tom Outland had not come back again through the garden door (as he had so often done in dreams!), but another boy had: the boy the Professor had long ago left behind him in Kansas, in the Solomon Valley—the original, unmodified Godfrey St. Peter.

===Chinese===
圣彼得从前总是嘲笑那些谈论「白日梦」的人，正如他嘲笑那些天真地承认自己「富有想象力」的人。他这一生，心智向来是积极用事的。他不在工作、也不在积极地寻乐时，就去睡觉。他从来没有那段半睡半醒的过渡时分。可如今他却享受起这种脑子半醒半寐地放空的状态来，仿佛那是一种新生的感官，姗姗来迟，一如智齿。他发现自己可以在湖边那道沙咀上一躺几个钟头，看那七棵纹丝不动的松树把阳光饮尽。傍晚吃过饭，他也能无所事事地坐着看星星，同样地一动不动。他正培养着一种全新的精神上的消遣——并且正享受着一段新的友谊。汤姆·奥特兰并没有再从花园那扇门里走回来（虽然他在梦里曾多少次这样走进来！），可另一个少年却走回来了：那是教授很久以前在堪萨斯、在所罗门河谷丢下的那个少年——那个原本的、未经改塑的戈弗雷·圣彼得。

===Original===
This boy and he had meant, back in those faraway days, to live some sort of life together and to share good and bad fortune. They had not shared together, for the reason that they were unevenly matched. The young St. Peter who went to France to try his luck, had a more active mind than the twin he left behind in the Solomon Valley. After his adoption into the Thierault household, he remembered that other boy very rarely, in moments of homesickness. After he met Lillian Ornsley, St. Peter forgot that boy had ever lived.

===Chinese===
在那些遥远的岁月里，这个少年和他本打算相依着过某种生活，同甘共苦。可他们并没有真正共享什么，原因是两人并不般配。那个去法国闯天下的年轻的圣彼得，比他留在所罗门河谷的那个孪生的自己心思更活络。自从被蒂罗一家收养之后，他只是在想家的当口才偶或记起那另一个少年。等到他遇见了莉莲·奥恩斯利，圣彼得便把那个少年曾经存在过这件事都忘干净了。

===Original===
But now that the vivid consciousness of an earlier state had come back to him, the Professor felt that life with this Kansas boy, little as there had been of it, was the realest of his lives, and that all the years between had been accidental and ordered from the outside. His career, his wife, his family, were not his life at all, but a chain of events which had happened to him. All these things had nothing to do with the person he was in the beginning.

===Chinese===
可如今，那种属于更早状态的鲜活意识又回到了他身上，教授便觉得，与这个堪萨斯少年共度的那段生活，尽管为时甚短，却是他所有人生里最真实的一段，而其间流逝的这些年月全是偶然的、是自外强加于他的。他的事业、他的妻子、他的家人，根本不是他的人生，而不过是一串发生在他身上的事件。这一切，都和他最初那个人毫无干系。

===Original===
The man he was now, the personality his friends knew, had begun to grow strong during adolescence, during the years when he was always consciously or unconsciously conjugating the verb “to love”—in society and solitude, with people, with books, with the sky and open country, in the lonesomeness of crowded city streets. When he met Lillian, it reached its maturity. From that time to this, existence had been a catching at handholds. One thing led to another and one development brought on another, and the design of his life had been the work of this secondary social man, the lover. It had been shaped by all the penalties and responsibilities of being and having been a lover. Because there was Lillian, there must be marriage and a salary. Because there was marriage, there were children. Because there were children, and fervour in the blood and brain, books were born as well as daughters. His histories, he was convinced, had no more to do with his original ego than his daughters had; they were a result of the high pressure of young manhood.

===Chinese===
他如今所是的这个人，他的朋友们所认识的那个他，是从青春期开始强壮起来的——在那许多年里，他总是有意无意地在变位着「去爱」这个动词——在人群中也在独处时，与人，与书，与天空和旷野，在熙攘的城市街道的孤寂之中。到他遇见莉莲时，这一切臻于成熟。从那时直到如今，他的生存不过是一路攀附可供抓手之处。一桩事引出另一桩事，一种发展带起另一种发展，而他一生的图样，便是这第二个社会性的自我、这个恋人一手造成的。它被身为恋人、曾经是恋人所带来的一切惩罚与责任所塑成。因为有了莉莲，就必须有婚姻和一份薪水。因为有了婚姻，就有了孩子。因为有了孩子，又兼血里和脑子里那股热忱，于是在女儿之外又诞生了书。他深信，他那些史书与他原本的那个自我，并不比他的女儿们与那个自我关系更大；它们都是青年时代那股高压之下的产物。

===Original===
The Kansas boy who had come back to St. Peter this summer was not a scholar. He was a primitive. He was only interested in earth and woods and water. Wherever sun sunned and rain rained and snow snowed, wherever life sprouted and decayed, places were alike to him. He was not nearly so cultivated as Tom’s old cliff-dwellers must have been—and yet he was terribly wise. He seemed to be at the root of the matter; Desire under all desires, Truth under all truths. He seemed to know, among other things, that he was solitary and must always be so; he had never married, never been a father. He was earth, and would return to earth. When white clouds blew over the lake like bellying sails, when the seven pine-trees turned red in the declining sun, he felt satisfaction and said to himself merely: “That is right.” Coming upon a curly root that thrust itself across his path, he said: “That is it.” When the maple-leaves along the street began to turn yellow and waxy, and were soft to the touch—like the skin on old faces—he said: “That is true; it is time.” All these recognitions gave him a kind of sad pleasure.

===Chinese===
今年夏天回到圣彼得身边的那个堪萨斯少年，并不是个学者。他是个原始人。他只对土地、林木和水感兴趣。凡是太阳照耀、雨水降落、大雪纷飞之处，凡是生命萌发与腐朽之所，这些地方在他看来都一样。他远不及汤姆那些古老的崖居人那样有教养——然而他却可怕地睿智。他似乎触及了一切事物的根底；是一切欲望之下的欲望，一切真相之下的真相。他仿佛知道许多事，其中之一便是：他是孤独的，而且必将永远如此；他从未娶妻，从未做过父亲。他是泥土，终将归于泥土。当白云如鼓满了风的船帆般从湖上飘过，当那七棵松树在夕阳里转成红色，他便感到满足，只自言自语道：「这就对了。」撞见一条虬结的树根横过长路，他便说：「就是这样。」当街边的枫叶开始泛黄、发涩、摸起来软软的——像老人脸上的皮肤——他便说：「这是真的；是时候了。」所有这些认取，都给他带来一种淡淡的、带着愁绪的愉悦。

===Original===
When he was not dumbly, deeply recognizing, he was bringing up out of himself long-forgotten, unimportant memories of his early childhood, of his mother, his father, his grandfather. His grandfather, old Napoleon Godfrey, used to go about lost in profound, continuous meditation, sometimes chuckling to himself. Occasionally, at the family dinner-table, the old man would try to rouse himself, from motives of politeness, and would ask some kindly question—nearly always absurd and often the same one he had asked yesterday. The boys used to shout with laughter and wonder what profound matters could require such deep meditation, and make a man speak so foolishly about what was going on under his very eyes. St. Peter thought he was beginning to understand what the old man had been thinking about, though he himself was but fifty-two, and Napoleon had been well on in his eighties. There are only a few years, at the last, in which man can consider his estate, and he thought he might be quite as near the end of his road as his grandfather had been in those days.

===Chinese===
当他不是在默默地、深深地认取什么的时候，他便从自己心底打捞出一些早已淡忘、无足轻重的儿时记忆，关于他母亲、他父亲、他祖父的记忆。他的祖父，老拿破仑·戈弗雷，从前常常一边走来走去，一边沉浸在绵长深沉的冥想里，有时还独自轻声发笑。偶尔在一家人的饭桌上，老人出于礼貌会强打精神振作一下，问上一句和气的话——那话几乎总是荒唐的，而且往往和昨天问的一模一样。孩子们总爱哄堂大笑，纳闷究竟是什么了不得的大事需要这样深长的冥想，又能让一个人对眼下发生的事说得那样糊涂。圣彼得想，他大概开始明白老人当年在想些什么了，虽说他自己才五十二岁，而拿破仑当年已是八十好几的人了。一个人到了最后，能够静下心来省察自己一生的年月本来就不多，而他觉得自己离那条路的尽头，也许并不比祖父当年更远。

===Original===
The Professor knew, of course, that adolescence grafted a new creature into the original one, and that the complexion of a man’s life was largely determined by how well or ill his original self and his nature as modified by sex rubbed on together.

===Chinese===
教授当然明白，青春期会在原本那个人身上嫁接出一个新的造物，而一个人一生的色调，很大程度上取决于他原本的自我与被性别所改塑的那部分天性，二者是相安还是相磨。

===Original===
What he had not known was that, at a given time, that first nature could return to a man, unchanged by all the pursuits and passions and experiences of his life; untouched even by the tastes and intellectual activities which have been strong enough to give him distinction among his fellows and to have made for him, as they say, a name in the world. Perhaps this reversion did not often occur, but he knew it had happened to him, and he suspected it had happened to his grandfather. He did not regret his life, but he was indifferent to it. It seemed to him like the life of another person.

===Chinese===
他所不知道的是，到了某个时刻，那最初的天性竟会回到一个人身上来，丝毫不变于他毕生的种种追求、激情和阅历；甚至连那些强烈到足以使他在同侪中崭露头角、据说为他挣下了一个「名声」的趣味和智识活动，也触它不动。也许这种回返并不常有，可他知道这事确确实实发生在自己身上了，而且他疑心当年也发生在祖父身上。他并不懊悔自己的一生，可他对它已无所谓了。在他看来，那就像是另一个人的人生。

===Original===
Along with other states of mind which attended his realization of the boy Godfrey, came a conviction (he did not see it coming, it was there before he was aware of its approach) that he was nearing the end of his life. This conviction took its place so quietly, seemed so matter-of-fact, that he gave it little thought. But one day, when he realized that all the while he was preparing for the fall term he didn’t in the least believe he would be alive during the fall term, he thought he might better see a doctor.

===Chinese===
伴随那种觉知到少年戈弗雷存在而来的种种心境，还有一种确信（他没见它走近，等他察觉时它早已在那儿了）：他已临近自己一生的尽头。这确信来得那样悄然，显得那样理所当然，他几乎没怎么把它放在心上。可有一天，他意识到，自己一边为秋季学期做着种种准备，心里却压根儿不相信自己能活到秋季学期，他便想，自己还是去看一趟医生为好。

### III / III

===Original===
The family doctor knew all about St. Peter. It was summer, moreover, and he had plenty of time. He devoted several mornings to the Professor and made tests of the most searching kind. In the end he of course told St. Peter there was nothing the matter with him.

===Chinese===
家庭医生对圣彼得的一切了如指掌。况且又是夏天，他有大把的闲暇。他一连花了几个上午为教授诊治，做了种种最严密的检查。到末了，他当然告诉圣彼得，他什么毛病也没有。

===Original===
“What made you come to me, any discomfort or pain?”

“None. I simply feel tired all the time.”

Dr. Dudley shrugged. “So do I! Sleep well?”

“Almost too much.”

“Eat well?”

“In every sense of the word, well. I am my own chef.”

“Always a gourmet, and never anything wrong with your digestive tract! I wish you’d ask me to dine with you some night. Any of that sherry left?”

“A little. I use it plentifully.”

“I’ll bet you do! But why did you think there was something wrong with you? Low in your mind?”

“No, merely low in energy. Enjoy doing nothing. I came to you from a sense of duty.”

“How about travel?”

“I shrink from the thought of it. As I tell you, I enjoy doing nothing.”

“Then do it! There’s nothing the matter with you. Follow your inclination.”

===Chinese===
「是什么让你来找我？哪里不舒服，还是疼？」

「没有。我只是成天觉得累。」

达德利医生耸耸肩。「我也一样！睡得好吗？」

「简直睡得太多了。」

「胃口呢？」

「字面意义上的好。我自己给自己当厨子。」

「一向是个美食家，消化道又从来没出过半点毛病！我真盼着你哪天晚上请我吃顿饭。雪利酒还有剩的吗？」

「还有一点。我喝得挺大方。」

「我猜也是！可你为什么觉得自己不对劲儿？情绪低落吗？」

「不，只是元气不足。什么也不干，正合我意。我上你这儿来，是出于尽责。」

「那出去旅行一趟怎么样？」

「一想起来就发怵。我跟你说过了，我什么也不干，正合我意。」

「那就由着它去！你什么毛病也没有。顺着你自己的性子就是了。」

===Original===
St. Peter went home well satisfied. He did not mention to Dr. Dudley the real reason for his asking for a medical examination. One doesn’t mention such things. The feeling that he was near the conclusion of his life was an instinctive conviction, such as we have when we waken in the dark and know at once that it is near morning; or when we are walking across the country and suddenly know that we are near the sea.

===Chinese===
圣彼得心满意足地回了家。他并没有向达德利医生说出自己要求体检的真正缘由。这种事是不会说出口的。那种觉得自己已临近人生尽头的感觉，是一种出于本能的确信，就像我们在黑暗中醒来，一下子便知道天快亮了；又像我们正徒步穿过原野，忽然就知道大海近了。

===Original===
Letters came every week from France. Lillian and Louie alternated, so that one or the other got off a letter to him on every fast boat. Louie told him that wherever they went, when they had an especially delightful day, they bought him a present. At Trouville, for instance, they had laid in dozens of the brilliant rubber casquettes he liked to wear when he went swimming. At Aix-les-Bains they found a gorgeous dressing-gown for him in a Chinese shop. St. Peter was happy in his mind about them all. He was glad they were there, and that he was here. Their generous letters, written when there were so many pleasant things to do, certainly deserved more than one reading. He used to carry them out to the lake to read them over again. After coming out of the water he would lie on the sand, holding them in his hand, but somehow never taking his eyes off the pine-trees, appliquéd against the blue water, and their ripe yellow cones, dripping with gum and clustering on the pointed tips like a mass of golden bees in swarming-time. Usually he carried his letters home unread.

===Chinese===
每个礼拜都有信从法国寄来。莉莲和路易轮流着写，每一班快船总有一人给他寄出一封信。路易告诉他，他们不论到了哪儿，只要那一天过得格外快活，就会给他买上一件礼物。譬如在特鲁维尔，他们便买下了一打那种色彩鲜亮的橡胶泳帽，那是他下水时爱戴的。在艾克斯莱班，他们又在一家中国铺子里为他寻到一件华丽的晨衣。圣彼得心里头为他们欢喜。他庆幸他们在那边，也庆幸自己在这边。他们写来的信是那样宽厚，写的时候明明有那么多赏心乐事可做，这些信自然值得一读再读。他常把信揣到湖边去重读。从水里出来之后，他会躺在沙上，信握在手里，可不知怎的眼睛却总是离不开那些松树——它们贴着蓝湛湛的湖水立着，熟透了的黄色松球往下滴着松脂，一簇簇聚在尖梢上，像一群分蜂时节的金色蜜蜂。通常，他把信又原封不动地揣回了家。

===Original===
His family wrote constantly about their plans for next summer, when they were going to take him over with them. Next summer? The Professor wondered.… Sometimes he thought he would like to drive up in front of Notre Dame, in Paris, again, and see it standing there like the Rock of Ages, with the frail generations breaking about its base. He hadn’t seen it since the war.

===Chinese===
家里人不歇地写信谈他们明年夏天的打算，说那时要带他一同过去。明年夏天？教授在心里纳闷。……有时他想，要是能再乘车到巴黎圣母院的正门前，再看它矗立在那里，像万古磐石一般，任柔弱的世代在它脚下粉碎如浪花，那该多好。自打战争爆发，他还没再见过它。

===Original===
But if he went anywhere next summer, he thought it would be down into Outland’s country, to watch the sunrise break on sculptured peaks and impassable mountain passes—to look off at those long, rugged, untamed vistas dear to the American heart. Dear to all hearts, probably—at least, calling to all. Else why had his grandfather’s grandfather, who had tramped so many miles across Europe into Russia with the Grande Armée, come out to the Canadian wilderness to forget the chagrin of his Emperor’s defeat?

===Chinese===
可如果明年夏天他真要去什么地方，他想那该是下到奥特兰的国度去，看朝阳在雕凿般的群峰与不可逾越的山隘上绽开——去眺望那些长长的、崎岖的、未被驯服的远景，那是美国人心之所系的远景。多半也是所有人之心所系吧——至少，是向所有人发出呼唤的。若不如此，他的曾祖之父当年随大军徒步穿过欧洲、远入俄国那么些英里，又何必跑到加拿大的荒野里来，好忘掉他那位皇帝兵败所带来的愠恨呢？

### IV / IV

===Original===
The fall term of the university opened, and now the Professor went to his lectures instead of to the lake. He supposed he did his work; he heard no complaints from his assistants, and the students seemed interested. He found, however, that he wasn’t willing to take the trouble to learn the names of several hundred new students. It wasn’t worth while. He felt that his relations with them would be of short duration.

===Chinese===
大学的秋季学期开学了，如今教授是去上课，而不是去湖边了。他想必还是把活儿干了；他没听到助教们有什么怨言，学生们看上去也颇有兴致。然而他发觉，自己并不情愿费那个劲去记下几百个新生的名字。不值得。他觉得自己和他们之间的缘分不会长久。

===Original===
The McGregors got home from their vacation in Oregon, and Scott was much amused to find the Professor so doggedly anchored in the old house.

“It never struck me, Doctor, that you were a man who would be keeping up two establishments. They’ll be coming home pretty soon, and then you’ll have to decide where you are going to live.”

“I can’t leave my study, Scott. That’s flat.”

“Don’t, then! Darn it, you’ve a right to two houses if you want ’em.”

===Chinese===
麦格雷戈一家从俄勒冈度假回来了，斯科特见教授这样死死地守在老房子里，觉得十分好笑。

「我可真没想到，博士，你是那种会同时维持两处家宅的人。他们过不了多久就要回来了，到那时候，你总得决定自己到底住哪儿。」

「我离不开我的书房，斯科特。就这么回事。」

「那就别搬嘛！见鬼，你要是想要两处房子，你完全有这个权利。」

===Original===
This encounter took place on the street in front of the house. The Professor went wearily upstairs and lay down on the couch, his refuge from this ever-increasing fatigue. He really didn’t see what he was going to do about the matter of domicile. He couldn’t make himself believe that he was ever going to live in the new house again. He didn’t belong there. He remembered some lines of a translation from the Norse he used to read long ago in one of his mother’s few books, a little two-volume Ticknor and Fields edition of Longfellow, in blue and gold, that used to lie on the parlour table:

===Chinese===
这番碰面是在屋前的街上发生的。教授疲惫不堪地上楼，在长沙发上躺了下来——那沙发是他躲避这日甚一日疲倦的栖身之所。他实在看不出，关于住处这桩事自己究竟该怎么办。他没法让自己相信，自己还会再去新宅子里住。他不属于那里。他想起了从前译自古北欧诗的几行诗，那是很久以前他常读的，就在他母亲那寥寥几本书里的一本中——提克诺与菲尔兹出版公司出的那套朗费罗诗集小两卷本，蓝底金字，从前总是搁在客厅的桌子上：

===Original===
> For thee a house was built
 Ere thou wast born;
 For thee a mould was made
 Ere thou of woman earnest.

===Chinese===
> 早在你降生之前，
 一所房子已为你建成；
 早在你出自妇人之前，
 一副模子已为你造就。

===Original===
Lying on his old couch, he could almost believe himself in that house already. The sagging springs were like the sham upholstery that is put in coffins. Just the equivocal American way of dealing with serious facts, he reflected. Why pretend that it is possible to soften that last hard bed?

===Chinese===
躺在他这张旧沙发上，他几乎可以相信自己已经置身那所房子里了。那些塌陷的弹簧，活像棺材里衬的那种假软垫。这就是美国人含糊其辞地应付严肃事实的法子，他想。何必装作那最后一张硬床也能被软化呢？

===Original===
He could remember a time when the loneliness of death had terrified him, when the idea of it was insupportable. He used to feel that if his wife could but lie in the same coffin with him, his body would not be so insensible that the nearness of hers would not give it comfort. But now he thought of eternal solitude with gratefulness; as a release from every obligation, from every form of effort. It was the Truth.

===Chinese===
他还记得有过那样一段时日，死亡的那种孤独曾叫他恐惧万分，那一念简直不堪承受。他从前总觉得，要是妻子能和他同卧一棺，他的身子总不至麻木到感觉不到她的贴近、从中得到几分安慰。可如今他想到永恒的寂灭，心里竟怀着感激；把它当作从一切义务、一切形式的劳碌之中解脱出来。那便是真相。

===Original===
One morning, just as St. Peter was leaving the house to go to his classroom, the postman handed him two letters, one addressed in Lillian’s hand and one in Louie’s. He put them into his pocket. The feel of them disturbed him. They were of a suspicious thinness—as if they didn’t contain amusing gossip, but announced sudden decisions. He set off down the street, sniffing the lake-cooled morning air and trying to overcome a feeling of nervous dread.

===Chinese===
一天早晨，圣彼得正要出门去教室，邮差递给他两封信，一封是莉莲的笔迹，一封是路易的。他把信揣进口袋。信封的触感让他心神不宁。它们薄得可疑——仿佛里头装的不是什么有趣的家长里短，而是宣布某个突然的决定。他沿街走去，一边嗅着被湖水沁凉的晨风，一边想压下心头那阵紧张的惧意。

===Original===
All the morning those two letters lay in his breast pocket. Though they were so light, their effect was to make him drop his shoulders and look woefully tired. The weather, too, had changed, come on suddenly hot and sultry at noon, as if getting ready for a storm. When his classes were over and he was back in his study again, St. Peter felt no interest in lunch. He took out the two letters and ripped them open with his forefinger to have it over. Yes, all plans were changed, and by the happiest of expectations. The family were hurrying home to prepare for the advent of a young Marsellus. They would sail on the sixteenth, on the *Berengaria*.

===Chinese===
整整一上午，那两封信就揣在他胸前的口袋里。它们虽轻，却压得他肩膀耷拉下来，一脸疲惫不堪的神气。天气也变了，正午时分骤然燠热窒闷起来，像是在酝酿一场暴雨。等他上完课回到书房，圣彼得一点吃午饭的心思也没有。他掏出那两封信，用食指一气划开封口，只想早点了结。果然，所有的盘算都变了，而且是因为一桩再可喜不过的期待。一家人正急着赶回家，好为小马塞勒斯的降生做准备。他们将于十六日登船，搭的是「伯伦加丽亚号」。

===Original===
Lillian added a postscript to the effect that by this same mail she was getting off a letter to Augusta, who would come to him for the keys of the new house. She would be the best person to open the house and arrange to have the cleaning done. She would take it entirely off his shoulders and see that everything was properly put in order.

===Chinese===
莉莲还附了一句，说是趁这同一班邮件，她也给奥古斯塔发了一封信，她会来向他取新宅的钥匙。要开宅子、张罗清扫，奥古斯塔是最合适不过的人选。她会把这副担子整个从他肩头接过去，把一切都料理得妥妥帖帖。

===Original===
They were sailing on the sixteenth, and this was the seventeenth; they were already on the water. The *Berengaria* was a five-day boat. St. Peter caught up his hat and light overcoat and started down the stairs. Halfway down, he stopped short, went back to his study, and softly shut the door behind him. He sat down, forgetting to take off his overcoat, though the afternoon was so hot and his face was damp with perspiration. He sat motionless, breathing unevenly, one dark hand lying clenched on his writing-table. There must, he was repeating to himself, there must be some way in which a man who had always tried to live up to his responsibilities could, when the hour of desperation came, avoid meeting his own family.

===Chinese===
他们是十六日启程的，而今天已是十七日；他们早已在海上。「伯伦加丽亚号」是五天的航程。圣彼得抓起帽子和薄外套，便往楼下走。走到一半，他猛地煞住脚，折回书房，轻轻把门带上。他坐下来，竟忘了脱外套，尽管那个下午酷热难当，他脸上早已汗湿。他一动不动地坐着，呼吸不匀，一只黝黑的手攥成拳，搁在书桌上。他一遍遍地在心里念叨：一定有的，一定有某种办法，能让一个一向尽力尽责的人，在绝望来临的那一刻，得以避开他自己的家人。

===Original===
He loved his family, he would make any sacrifice for them, but just now he couldn’t live with them. He must be alone. That was more necessary to him than anything had ever been, more necessary, even, than his marriage had been in his vehement youth. He could not live with his family again—not even with Lillian. Especially not with Lillian! Her nature was intense and positive; it was like a chiselled surface, a die, a stamp upon which he could not be beaten out any longer. If her character were reduced to an heraldic device, it would be a hand (a beautiful hand) holding flaming arrows—the shafts of her violent loves and hates, her clear-cut ambitions.

===Chinese===
他爱他的家人，他愿为他们作出任何牺牲，可此时此刻他没法和他们住在一起。他必须独处。这于他比任何事都更要紧，甚至比他当年那热烈的青春里婚姻本身还更要紧。他再也不能和家人同住了——哪怕是和莉莲也不行。尤其是不能和莉莲！她的天性浓烈而鲜明；那就像一处凿刻过的平面，一枚钢模，一方印章，他已经再也无法被放在上面捶打成形了。倘若把她的性格化为一枚纹章，那该是一只手（一只美丽的手）擎着燃烧的箭矢——那便是她那暴烈的爱与恨、她那轮廓分明的雄心的箭。

===Original===
“In great misfortunes,” he told himself, “people want to be alone. They have a right to be. And the misfortunes that occur within one are the greatest. Surely the saddest thing in the world is falling out of love—if once one has ever fallen in.”

===Chinese===
「人在大不幸之中，」他对自己说，「总想独处。他们有这个权利。而发生在一个人内心深处的不幸，才是最大的不幸。这世上最悲哀的事，莫过于不再爱了——倘若曾经爱过的话。」

===Original===
Falling out, for him, seemed to mean falling out of all domestic and social relations, out of his place in the human family, indeed.

===Chinese===
对于他而言，这种「不再」，似乎意味着从一切家庭与社会的关系里脱落出去，从他在人类这个大家庭里的位置上脱落出去。

===Original===
St. Peter did not go out of the house that afternoon. He did not leave his study. He sat at his desk with bent head, reviewing his life, trying to see where he had made his mistake, to account for the fact that he now wanted to run away from everything he had intensely cared for.

===Chinese===
那天下午圣彼得没有出门。他没有离开书房。他伏在书桌前，低着头，回溯自己的一生，想看清自己究竟在哪一步上走错了，想解释清楚这样一个事实：他如今竟想逃离他曾深爱过的一切。

===Original===
Late in the afternoon the heaviness of the air in the room drove him to the window. He saw that a storm was coming on. Great orange and purple clouds were blowing up from the lake, and the pine-trees over about the Physics laboratory were blacker than cypresses and looked contracted, as if they were awaiting something. The rain broke, and it turned cold.

===Chinese===
临近傍晚，屋里沉重的气压把他逼到了窗前。他看见一场风暴就要来了。大团橘黄与紫色的云从湖上翻卷而来，物理实验室那一带的松树黑得赛过柏树，看上去蜷缩着，仿佛在等候着什么。雨落了下来，天凉了。

===Original===
The rainstorm was over in half an hour, but a heavy blow had set in for the night. The wind would be a protection, he thought. Even Augusta would hardly come plodding up the stairs tonight It seemed strange to be dreading Augusta, but just now he did dread her. He believed he was safe, for tonight. Though it was only five o’clock, the sky was black, and the room was dusky and chilly. He lit the stove and lay down on the couch. The fire made a flickering pattern of light on the wall. He lay watching it, vacantly; without meaning to, he fell asleep. For a long while he slept deeply and peacefully. Then the wind, increasing in violence, disturbed him. He began to be aware of noises—things banging and slamming about. He turned over on his back and slept deeper still.

===Chinese===
暴雨半个钟头就过了，可一夜的大风却刮了起来。这样的风会是一道屏障，他想。今夜就连奥古斯塔也多半不会拖着步子爬上楼来了。怕起奥古斯塔来，似乎很奇怪，可此刻他确确实实怕她来。他相信自己今夜是安全的。虽才五点钟，天已漆黑，屋里又暗又冷。他生了炉子，在沙发上躺下。炉火在墙上投下明明灭灭的光影。他茫然地躺着看那光影；不知不觉便睡着了。有很长一段时间，他睡得又沉又安稳。后来风势愈猛，惊动了他。他开始觉察到种种声响——是什么东西乒乒乓乓地乱响。他翻了个身，仰面朝天，睡得更沉了。

---

===Original===
When St. Peter at last awoke, the room was pitch-black and full of gas. He was cold and numb, felt sick and rather dazed. The long-anticipated coincidence had happened, he realized. The storm had blown the stove out and the window shut. The thing to do was to get up and open the window. But suppose he did not get up—? How far was a man required to exert himself against accident? How would such a case be decided under English law? He hadn’t lifted his hand against himself—was he required to lift it for himself?

===Chinese===
等到圣彼得终于醒来，屋里漆黑一片，满是煤气。他又冷又麻，恶心想吐，脑子昏昏沉沉。他意识到，那桩他早有预感的巧合终于发生了。风暴把炉子吹熄了，又把窗户吹得合上了。该做的事是爬起来去开窗。可要是他偏不起来呢——？一个人在意外面前，到底被要求使多大力气去自救？这样的案子，依照英国法会怎么判？他并没有举手加害自己——难道他被要求举手救助自己吗？

### V / V

===Original===
At midnight St. Peter was lying in his study, on his box-couch, covered up with blankets, a hot water bottle at his feet; he knew it was midnight, for the clock of Augusta’s church across the park was ringing the hour. Augusta herself was there in the room, sitting in her old sewing-chair by the kerosene lamp, wrapped up in a shawl. She was reading a little much-worn religious book that she always carried in her handbag. Presently he spoke to her.

===Chinese===
到了半夜，圣彼得正躺在书房的箱式沙发上，身上盖着毯子，脚边搁着一只热水袋；他知道正是半夜，因为公园对面奥古斯塔那座教堂的钟正在报时。奥古斯塔本人也在屋里，坐在煤油灯旁她那张旧缝纫椅上，身上裹着一条披肩。她正在读一本小小的、磨得卷了边的宗教小书，那是她总放在手提袋里随身带着的。过了一会儿，他开口和她说话。

===Original===
“Just when did you come in, Augusta?”

She got up and came over to him.

“Are you feeling comfortable, Doctor St. Peter?”

“Oh, very, thank you. When did you happen in?”

“Not any too soon, sir,” she said gravely, with a touch of reproof. “You never would take my cautions about that old stove, and it very nearly asphyxiated you. I was barely in time to pull you out.”

===Chinese===
「你到底是什么时候进来的，奥古斯塔？」

她起身走到他跟前。

「你觉得舒坦吗，圣彼得博士？」

「哦，很舒坦，谢谢你。你什么时候过来的？」

「来得可不早啦，先生，」她神情庄重地说，带着一点责备的口气。「我从前提醒你那旧炉子，你从来不肯听，这一回差点儿没把你呛死。我勉强算赶得及，把你拖了出来。」

===Original===
“You pulled me out, literally? Where to?”

“Into the hall. I came over in the storm to ask you for the keys of the new house—I didn’t get Mrs. St. Peter’s letter until I got home from work this evening, and I came right over. When I opened the front door I smelled gas, and I knew that stove had been up to its old tricks. I supposed you’d gone out and forgot to turn it off. When I got to the second floor I heard a fall overhead, and it flashed across me that you were up here and had been overcome. I ran up and opened the two windows at the head of the stairs and dragged you out into the wind. You were lying on the floor.” She lowered her voice. “It was perfectly frightful in here.”

===Chinese===
「你真把我拖出来了？拖到哪儿？」

「拖到门厅里。我是顶着那场暴风雨过来找你要新宅钥匙的——我今晚下了工回到家才收到圣彼得太太的信，便马上赶了过来。我一开前门就闻见了煤气味儿，就知道那炉子又在耍它的老把戏了。我原以为你出门去了，忘了关炉子。等我上到二楼，听见头顶上有人摔倒，脑子里一闪，便知道你就在楼上，已经中了煤气。我跑上来，推开楼梯口那两扇窗户，把你拖到风里头。你当时正躺在地上。」她压低了声音。「这屋里那样子，吓人得很。」

===Original===
“I seem to remember Dudley’s being here.”

“Yes, after I’d turned off the stove and opened everything up, I went next door and telephoned for Doctor Dudley. I thought I’d better not say what the trouble was, but I asked him to come at once, as you’d been taken ill. You soon came round, but you were flighty.” Augusta hurried over her recital. She was evidently embarrassed by the behaviour of the stove and the condition in which she had found him. It was an ugly accident, and she didn’t want the neighbours to know of it.

===Chinese===
「我好像记得达德利来过。」

「是的，等我把炉子关上、把所有的窗户都打开之后，我就到隔壁去给达德利医生打了电话。我想我还是不说究竟出了什么事为好，只请他马上过来一趟，说您病了。你不多会儿就缓过劲儿来了，只是人有点儿犯迷糊。」奥古斯塔把这番经过匆匆说完。她显然是为那炉子的行径、以及她发现他时的那副样子感到难为情。这是一桩难看的意外，她不愿意让邻居们知道。

===Original===
“You must have great presence of mind, Augusta, and a strong arm as well. You say you found me on the floor? I thought I was lying here on the couch. I remember waking up and smelling gas.”

“You were stupefied, but you must have got up and tried to get to the door before you were overcome. I was on the second floor when I heard you fall. I’d never heard anyone fall before, that I can remember, but I seemed to know just what it was.”

===Chinese===
「你真够镇定的，奥古斯塔，臂力也不小呢。你说你发现我时我在地上？我还以为自己是躺在这沙发上的呢。我只记得醒过来，闻见了煤气味儿。」

「你那会儿已经迷糊了，可你一定是爬起来想摸到门口，没等摸到就栽倒了。我听见你摔倒的时候正在二楼。就我记忆所及，我从来没听见过谁这样摔倒过，可我好像一下子就明白是怎么回事。」

===Original===
“I’m sorry to have given you a fright. I hope the gas hasn’t made your head ache.”

“All’s well that ends well, as they say. But I doubt if you ought to be talking, sir. Could you go to sleep again? I can stay till morning, if you prefer.”

“I’d be greatly obliged if you would stay the night with me, Augusta. It would be a comfort. I seem to feel rather lonely—for the first time in months.”

“That’s because your family are coming home. Very well, sir.”

===Chinese===
「叫你受惊了，真过意不去。但愿那煤气没叫你头疼。」

「结果好就一切都好，人家不都这么说嘛。不过我看您还是少说话为是，先生。您能再睡得着吗？您要是愿意，我可以一直守到天亮。」

「你要是肯留下来陪我过夜，那我太感激了，奥古斯塔。那会让我心里踏实些。我好像觉得有点儿孤单——这可是好几个月来头一遭。」

「那是因为您一家子要回来了。好吧，先生。」

===Original===
“You do a good deal of this sort of thing—watching and sitting up with people, don’t you?”

“Well, when I happen to be sewing in a house where there’s sickness, I am sometimes called upon.”

===Chinese===
「这种事——守夜、陪病人，你做得不少吧？」

「嗯，要是我恰好在哪户有病人的人家做针线活，有时就会被人请去帮忙。」

===Original===
Augusta sat down by the table and again took up her little religious book. St. Peter, with half-closed eyes, lay watching her—regarding in her humankind, as if after a definite absence from the world of men and women. If he had thought of Augusta sooner, he would have got up from the couch sooner. Her image would have at once suggested the proper action.

===Chinese===
奥古斯塔在桌边坐下，又拿起她那本小小的宗教书。圣彼得半阖着眼，躺着看她——借由她端详着整个人类，仿佛他才刚刚离开男男女女的世界、重新归来似的。要是他早一些想到奥古斯塔，他也就会早一些从沙发上爬起来。她的形象本该立刻提示他该做什么的。

===Original===
Augusta, he reflected, had always been a corrective, a remedial influence. When she sewed for them, she breakfasted at the house—that was part of the arrangement. She came early, often directly from church, and had her breakfast with the Professor, before the rest of the family were up. Very often she gave him some wise observation or discreet comment to begin the day with. She wasn’t at all afraid to say things that were heavily, drearily true, and though he used to wince under them, he hurried off with the feeling that they were good for him, that he didn’t have to hear such sayings half often enough. Augusta was like the taste of bitter herbs; she was the bloomless side of life that he had always run away from—yet when he had to face it, he found that it wasn’t altogether repugnant. Sometimes she used to telephone Mrs. St. Peter that she would be a day late, because there had been a death in the family where she was sewing just then, and she was “needed.” When she met him at the table the next morning, she would look just a little more grave than usual. While she ate a generous breakfast, she would reply to his polite questions about the illness or funeral with befitting solemnity, and then go readily to another topic, not holding the dolorous note. He used to say that he didn’t mind hearing Augusta announce these deaths which seemed to happen so frequently along her way, because her manner of speaking about it made death seem less uncomfortable. She hadn’t any of the sentimentality that comes from a fear of dying. She talked about death as she spoke of a hard winter or a rainy March, or any of the sadnesses of nature.

===Chinese===
奥古斯塔，他想，一向是一股矫正的力量、一种疗愈的影响。她来他们家做针线时，是在家里用早饭的——这是约定中的一部分。她来得早，常常是径直从教堂过来，趁家里其余的人还没起身，先和教授一起吃早饭。她往往会给他一句明达的话、或一得体的议论，作为这一天的开场。她一点也不怕说那些沉甸甸的、叫人听了发闷的大实话，虽然他听了常要皱眉，可匆匆出门时又觉得这些话于他有益，觉得这样的话语自己连一半也没听够。奥古斯塔像苦草药的味道；她是生命里那不开花的一面，他一向躲着她——可一旦不得不正视，他发觉它也并不全是叫人厌恶的。有时她会打电话给圣彼得太太，说自己要晚一天来，因为她做活的那户人家死了人，她「被需要着」。第二天早晨在饭桌上遇见他时，她的神情会比平日更凝重一点点。她一边饱饱地吃着早饭，一边得体而庄重地回答他关于那场病或葬礼的客套询问，然后便爽快地转到别的话题上去，并不在那份哀戚的调子上停留。他常说，他并不介意听奥古斯塔通报这些似乎总在她路途上频频发生的死讯，因为她谈吐之间，让死亡显得不那么难堪了。她身上丝毫没有那种源于怕死的感伤。她谈论死亡，就像她谈论一个严寒的冬天、一个多雨的三月，或者大自然任何一种悲凉之处一样。

===Original===
It occurred to St. Peter, as he lay warm and relaxed but undesirous of sleep, that he would rather have Augusta with him just now than anyone he could think of. Seasoned and sound and on the solid earth she surely was, and, for all her matter-of-factness and hard-handedness, kind and loyal. He even felt a sense of obligation toward her, instinctive, escaping definition, but real. And when you admitted that a thing was real, that was enough—now.

===Chinese===
圣彼得躺在那儿，浑身暖和、松弛，却不想睡，他忽然想到：此时此刻，他能想到的任何人，都不如奥古斯塔在他身边更叫他称心。她无疑是老练、硬朗、双脚踩在实地上的人，尽管她那样务实、那一双手那样粗糙，却心地忠厚、为人仗义。他甚至对她怀着一种亏欠之感，那是出于本能的、难以言传的，却是真实的。而一旦你承认一件事是真实的，那就够了——此刻而言。

===Original===
He didn’t, on being quite honest with himself, feel any obligations toward his family. Lillian had had the best years of his life, nearly thirty, and joyful years they had been, nothing could ever change that. But they were gone. His daughters had outgrown any great need of him. In certain wayward moods Kitty would always come to him. But Rosamond, on that shopping expedition in Chicago, had shown him how painful the paternal relation could be. There was still Augusta, however; a world full of Augustas, with whom one was outward bound.

===Chinese===
对自己彻底坦诚地说来，他对家人倒并不觉得有什么义务。莉莲已经拥有了他一生中最好的年华，将近三十年，而那些年是欢乐的年月，无论什么也改变不了这一点。可它们已经过去了。他的女儿们早已过了非他不可的年纪。凯蒂在某些任性发作的时候，总会来找他。可罗莎蒙德，在那次芝加哥的采购之行里，已经让他看到了父女之情可以变得多么难堪。不过，奥古斯塔还在；这世上满是奥古斯塔们，与他们一道，人才能启程向外的航程。

===Original===
All the afternoon he had sat there at the table where now Augusta was reading, thinking over his life, trying to see where he had made his mistake. Perhaps the mistake was merely in an attitude of mind. He had never learned to live without delight. And he would have to learn to, just as, in a Prohibition country, he supposed he would have to learn to live without sherry. Theoretically he knew that life is possible, may be even pleasant, without joy, without passionate griefs. But it had never occurred to him that he might have to live like that.

===Chinese===
整整一下午，他都坐在那张如今奥古斯塔正在那儿看书的桌子前，反复思索着自己的一生，想看清自己究竟在哪一步上走错了。也许那错误不过在于一种心境。他从未学会过在没有欢愉的日子里生活。而他如今非得学会不可，就像，他想，在一个禁酒的国家里，他非得学会在没有雪利酒的日子里过活一样。从理论上他知道，没有欢乐、没有浓烈的悲戚，生活仍是可能的，甚至可能是惬意的。可他从未想过，自己也许也得那样地过活。

===Original===
Though he had been low-spirited all summer, he told the truth when he told Dr. Dudley that he had not been melancholy. He had no more thought of suicide than he had thought of embezzling. He had always regarded it as a grave social misdemeanour—except when it occurred in very evil times, as a form of protest. Yet when he was confronted by accidental extinction, he had felt no will to resist, but had let chance take its way, as it had done with him so often. He did not remember springing up from the couch, though he did remember a crisis, a moment of acute, agonized strangulation.

===Chinese===
虽说整整一夏他都情绪低落，可他告诉达德利医生自己并非忧郁时，说的是实话。他起过自尽的念头，并不比他起过挪用公款的念头更多。他一向把那看作一桩严重的社会失德——除非它发生在非常黑暗的时世里，作为一种抗议。然而当一场意外的了断摆到他面前时，他却并无丝毫抵抗的意志，而是任由偶然自行其道，一如它从前对他那样常常所为。他记不起自己有没有从沙发上挣扎着爬起来过，不过他确曾记得有过一个紧要关头，有过一阵剧烈的、痛苦万分的窒息。

===Original===
His temporary release from consciousness seemed to have been beneficial. He had let something go—and it was gone: something very precious, that he could not consciously have relinquished, probably. He doubted whether his family would ever realize that he was not the same man they had said goodbye to; they would be too happily preoccupied with their own affairs. If his apathy hurt them, they could not possibly be so much hurt as he had been already. At least, he felt the ground under his feet. He thought he knew where he was, and that he could face with fortitude the *Berengaria* and the future.

===Chinese===
那一时的失去知觉，似乎反倒对他有益。他放走了某样东西——而它确已离他而去：某样极珍贵的东西，多半是他若有意识便绝不肯撒手的东西。他怀疑他的家人究竟会不会意识到，他已不是他们道别时的那个人了；他们只会太快乐地忙着各自的事情。纵使他的冷淡伤了他们的心，他们所受的伤也绝不可能比他已经受过的更深。至少，他感到了脚下有实地。他觉得自己知道身在何处，知道自己能够以坚忍去面对那艘「伯伦加丽亚号」和它所带来的未来。

===Original===
For Jan, because he likes narrative

===Chinese===
致简，因为他喜欢故事

===Original===
> "A turquoise set in silver; wasn't it? … Yes, a turquoise set in dull silver."

===Chinese===
> 「一块镶嵌在银子里的绿松石；是不是？……是的，一块镶嵌在暗银里的绿松石。」

## Book I The Family / 卷一 家庭

## Book III The Professor / 卷三 教授

## Book II Tom Outland's Story / 卷二 汤姆·奥特兰的故事
